  `dash-platform`'s and `dune`'s stubbed HTTP steps/normalization (both unreachable through
  `CapabilityRegistry` in M1, since both adapters' own `isAvailable()` is unconditionally
  `{ok: false}`) so a direct, out-of-band call still fails loudly rather than silently. Shared by
  both adapters instead of two near-identical local classes (DRY — the two stub adapters need
  byte-identical semantics, not independently-evolvable ones).
- **`dash-metrics.ts`** — `DASH_PLATFORM_ASSET = 'dash-platform'` + the `DASH_METRIC` map
  (`identitiesTotal`, `documentsTotal`, `dataContractsTotal`, `platformTotalCredits`,
  `shieldedPoolBalanceCredits`, `shieldedPoolShieldAmount`). These ids are reused **verbatim**
  from the pre-M0 `onchain-snapshotter` n8n workflow's own `Normalize` Code node
  (`n8n-workflows/exported/onchain-snapshotter.json`) — NOT invented here — so the M1 engine and
  the already-running n8n snapshotter agree on the same metric vocabulary for the same
  `platform-explorer` fields (DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT §1 "one metrics vocabulary" convention). Shared by
  `platform-explorer`, `dash-platform`, and `pg-history` (which reads back exactly these metric
  ids from `onchain.snapshots`) — a single source of truth instead of the same literal strings
  repeated three times, where a typo in one copy would silently fragment a metric's time series
  across two different ids.

### src/adapters/rpc-evm/index.ts, src/adapters/rpc-solana/index.ts (new, task 003-5, R-16/R-17 backend, OQ-1)

Two more LIVE `ProviderAdapter` factories (`createRpcEvmAdapter`/`createRpcSolanaAdapter`), same
factory/DI convention as the 003-4 adapters (`fetchImpl?`/`now?`, no `env` — both keyless). Both
serve `wallet.balances.native` (one chain each) via JSON-RPC over HTTP (`fetch`-based, through
`safeFetch`), backing the `onchain_wallet_balances` MCP tool's OQ-1 resolution (native-only
balances in M1 — ERC-20/SPL balances are an explicit backlog item, ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2).

- **`rpc-evm`**: `eth_getBalance` JSON-RPC call, hosts confirmed live 2026-07-22 —
  `ethereum-rpc.publicnode.com` (primary) + `eth.drpc.org` (fallback), both in `hosts` (the SSRF
  allowlist doubles as this adapter's OWN endpoint list). **Within-adapter fallback**: its own
  HTTP step tries each `ENDPOINTS` entry in order, catching any failure (non-2xx or a thrown
  error) and moving to the next host, only throwing after all are exhausted — this is DIFFERENT
  from `CapabilityRegistry`'s cross-adapter fallback (R-11): `wallet.balances.native` on ethereum
  has only `rpc-evm` in its route's `adapterIds`, so a fully-dead JSON-RPC layer needs its own
  retry inside this one adapter, not a second registered adapter id. `normalize()` converts the
  hex-wei `result` to an exact decimal string via `BigInt(...).toString(10)` — **never**
  `Number()` (DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT §1.7 — wei routinely exceeds the safe 2^53 integer range).
  `amountNum` is still populated as a documented lossy display projection.
- **`rpc-solana`**: `getBalance` JSON-RPC call, single confirmed live host
  `api.mainnet-beta.solana.com` (2026-07-22) — **no fallback host** in M1 (ARCHITECTURE.md §11: a
  second keyless Solana RPC candidate needs its own separate live probe before being added; this
  is a documented single point of failure with retry, not a hot-swap, unlike `rpc-evm`'s two
  hosts). **Documented vendor limitation** (not an engine bug): unlike `eth_getBalance`,
  Solana's `getBalance` wire response carries `result.value` as a JSON **number**, not a
  hex/decimal string — so a lamport balance above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` (~9.007M SOL) has
  already lost precision by the time `response.json()` parses it, before this adapter ever sees
  it; `amountRaw` is still emitted as a string per convention, but merely reflects that
  already-lossy number for balances past that threshold. This is called out explicitly in the
  module's own docstring rather than silently accepted.
- Both `isAvailable()` unconditionally `{ok: true}` (keyless, no env precondition) and `costOf()`
  `{credits: 0}`.

**Input hardening (adversarial cycle 1, fixes E/F) — both tightened the "raw wire value → exact
numeric string" boundary, never trusting the vendor response's shape blindly:**

- **`rpc-evm` (fix E):** the hex-balance guard is now `HEX_BALANCE_RE = /^0x[0-9a-fA-F]+$/`
  (requires AT LEAST ONE hex digit after the `0x` prefix) — the PREVIOUS check
  (`typeof === 'string' && startsWith('0x')`) accepted the bare string `"0x"` (zero digits), which
  `BigInt("0x")` throws a raw, unhelpful `SyntaxError` on ("Cannot convert 0x to a BigInt") instead
  of this adapter's own clear `rpc-evm.normalize: invalid balance hex in "result": ...` error.
  `test/rpc-evm.contract.test.ts` gained a case asserting `result: "0x"` throws that exact message,
  not a `SyntaxError`.
- **`rpc-solana` (fix F):** `result.value` (lamports) is now validated as a non-negative SAFE
  integer (`Number.isInteger(lamports) && lamports >= 0 && lamports <= Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`)
  BEFORE it's ever handed to `String()` — a fractional value (never a valid lamport count), a
  negative value, or a value already past `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` (silently imprecise, per this
  module's own vendor-limitation docstring above) all get a loud `rpc-solana.normalize: invalid
lamports value in "result.value": ...` error instead of silently propagating a wrong/misleading
  `amountRaw`. `test/rpc-solana.contract.test.ts` gained an `it.each` over `1.5`/`-1`/`1e21`, each
  asserting that exact error message.

**Bounded error messages (post-M1 polish, cheap-fix backlog item 5):** both adapters' error paths
used to embed the ENTIRE raw JSON-RPC response envelope via a bare `JSON.stringify(raw)` (plus,
separately, `JSON.stringify(raw.error)` for the JSON-RPC-error branch) directly into a thrown
`Error`'s message — unbounded, up to `safeFetch`'s own `DEFAULT_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` (10MB) if a
misbehaving/malicious endpoint sent a huge body. Both call sites in both files now go through the
new shared `stringifyTruncated()` helper (`../stringify-truncated.js`, see its own section below) —
500 chars + a `…[truncated]` marker, only when truncation actually happened. One new regression
test per file (`test/rpc-evm.contract.test.ts`/`test/rpc-solana.contract.test.ts`): a ~50KB
oversized fake response still produces a bounded (well under 1KB), clearly-truncated error message.

### Adapters after TASK-006 task 006-5 (R-54/R-57) — private chain maps removed

Every adapter now answers "can I serve this chain?" with a `chainSupport(ChainInfo)` predicate read
from the REGISTRY, and reads its vendor's own chain naming from the same row. What disappeared:

| Adapter                  | Deleted                                             | Replaced by                                                                     |
| ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `defillama`              | `type SupportedChain`, `CHAIN_TVL_KEY` (2 entries)  | `chain.vendors.defillama` (458 rows)                                            |
| `coingecko`              | inline `chain !== 'ethereum' && chain !== 'solana'` | `chain.vendors.coingecko` — used for the URL **and** the `detail_platforms` key |
| `dexscreener`            | `type SupportedChain`, `NATIVE_QUERY`               | `chain.nativeSymbol` + `chain.vendors.dexscreener`                              |
| `rpc-evm` / `rpc-solana` | `chain !== 'ethereum'` / `!== 'solana'`             | `family` + a curated `rpcHosts`                                                 |
| `providers.config.ts`    | **every per-route `chains` literal**                | the coverage matrix                                                             |

- **`providers.config.ts` route literals could not simply be deleted.** The two
  `wallet.balances.native` routes differed ONLY by `chains`, and `resolve()` took the FIRST
  matching route — dropping the literal would have sent Solana balances to the EVM adapter.
  `resolve()` now collects the adapters of EVERY route matching the capability and skips those
  whose `chainSupport()` says no; that per-adapter skip is exactly what the route literal used to
  do. A skipped adapter is deliberately NOT recorded in `tried`: "does not serve this chain" is a
  coverage fact, and listing it would make it look like an outage.
- **`ChainSchema` widened from `z.enum([...])` to a slug string** (`src/types/chain.ts`), with a
  recorded deviation from ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2's CAIP-2-branded schema: domain types carry the
  SLUG because R-59d forbids changing tool response shapes, and `onchain_get_token` has always
  answered `chain: "ethereum"`. CAIP-2 remains the registry's primary key and the value §4.2.2
  wants in the cache key (task 006-6 does that at the tool boundary). Both are canonical; neither
  is an alias, which is what §4.2.2 actually requires.
- **`address.ts`'s legacy string arm now RESOLVES unknown slugs** instead of defaulting to
  `'other'`. Once `Chain` became `string`, a bare `'berachain'` would have missed the three-entry
  legacy map and silently skipped EIP-55 canonicalization — a wrong answer that looks right, and
  the worst possible failure for something feeding a cache key.
- **`nansen` deliberately keeps the M2 scope** (`ethereum`/`solana`), and `type NansenChain` and
  the `raw.chain === 'solana'` case-folding in `normalize.ts` are left in place. Task 006-9 derives
  the real coverage from the committed OpenAPI spec, where it differs PER CAPABILITY and a
  composite capability is covered by the INTERSECTION of its sub-calls. Widening here would add 8
  chains on which the call half-succeeds AFTER spending credits. Deferring is the point, not an
  oversight.
- **CHANGED EXPECTATIONS (named for task 006-10):** `capabilities()` no longer carries a `chains`
  array in `defillama`/`coingecko`/`dexscreener` (three test cases rewritten); contract tests now
  resolve the fixture's chain slug into a `ChainInfo` before calling `normalize()`, because the
  adapters' private fetch-result shape carries a resolved chain (every expected OUTPUT unchanged);
  `ChainSchema` no longer rejects an unknown chain (`types.test.ts`).

### rpcHosts + the SSRF perimeter (TASK-006 task 006-8, R-56) — security-critical

`rpc-evm` now takes BOTH its endpoint list and the allowlist it hands `safeFetch` from **this
chain's** `rpcHosts` row — per chain, never merged, so one chain's endpoint can never be used to
reach another's. `safeFetch` itself is untouched: only the SOURCE of the allowlist changed, not the
mechanism.

- **`rpcHosts` is the one registry column the generator may not fill.** `chainid.network` publishes
  RPC endpoints for 2660 chains; importing them would mean the set of hosts we make outgoing
  requests to is decided by a third party over the network. The generator only _proposes_
  candidates in its report (`row.rpcHosts` is read, never assigned — a test greps for it).
- **19 of 458 chains curated** (2026-07-26). Criterion: highest-TVL EVM chains for which
  `chainid.network` offers a keyless https endpoint; each probed with `eth_chainId`, and only those
  returning the EXPECTED chain id were admitted.
- **One candidate was rejected by hand, and it is the argument for the whole rule.**
  `hyperliquid-l1` (chainId 999): its only live candidate was `gwan-ssl.wandevs.org` — a **Wanchain**
  domain. chainId 999 is historically claimed by both chains, so `eth_chainId` returns exactly the
  expected value and **the automated check passes**. Hyperliquid balance queries would have been
  answered by Wanchain. A machine can verify "responded with the right chain id"; it cannot verify
  "is the right chain".
- **A chain with `rpcHosts: null` is UNCOVERED, not broken**: `chainSupport()` reports it and the
  coverage gate refuses before any network attempt, rather than letting the call time out against a
  host nobody approved. A test asserts `fetchImpl` is never called.
- **Curation survives resync** — the generator carries `rpcHosts` forward and never erases it
  (asserted by test and re-verified against the live catalogs after curating).

### scripts/gen-nansen-coverage.ts + src/adapters/nansen/chain-coverage.ts (new, TASK-006 task 006-9, R-58)

Nansen's chain coverage, derived from the COMMITTED OpenAPI spec — the same "read the spec we
already paid for, emit code, review the diff" technique M2 uses for the `costOf()` cost table.

- **A COMPOSITE capability is covered by the INTERSECTION of its sub-calls, never the union.**
  `smart-money.flows` is ONE `fetch()` issuing TWO requests (`/smart-money/netflow`, 17 chains, and
  `/tgm/holders`, 25). The union would add chains where the first sub-call succeeds, the second is
  refused by the vendor, and the credits for the first are already gone — the DF-1 failure class
  that cost 35 real credits to find once. Result: `smart-money.flows` 17, `token.risk` 25,
  `entity.labels` 25, exhaustive-labels tier 19.
- **`chainSupport(chain, capability)` gained the capability argument for exactly this reason.**
  Coverage is a property of the PAIR. A predicate blind to the capability could answer only with a
  union (over-claiming, and the extra chains burn credits before failing) or an intersection
  (under-claiming). Adapters whose coverage does not vary ignore the argument.
- **The "half-succeeding" set is 7 of OUR chains, not the 8 the plan quotes** — and the gap is
  instructive: 8 counts NANSEN's tokens, where `hyperevm` and `hyperliquid` are two names for one
  chain of ours, and that chain IS covered for flows via `hyperevm`. Counting in vendor tokens
  overstates exposure by one. Both numbers are recorded in the evidence file.
- **`vendors.nansen` holds the VENDOR's token, not our slug** (`bsc`→`bnb`, `op-mainnet`→
  `optimism`, `iota-evm`→`iotaevm`). 25 chains mapped; an unmapped chain is uncovered for every
  capability, and an unknown capability covers nothing (fail-closed).
- **Live spot-check, 5 credits** (59→54, budget ≤6): `POST /tgm/indicators` on `base` — a chain
  OUTSIDE the M2 pair, chosen because re-verifying what already worked proves nothing. HTTP 200
  with populated indicators. Evidence:
  `docs/onchain-analytics/raw/nansen-chain-spotcheck-2026-07-26.json`.
- **Recorded deviation from R-58a** ("a run with the key"): the spec already carries the fact per
  endpoint, so sweeping 25 chains live would spend credits to reach the same conclusion. With no
  key the spot-check is skipped and the state is `spec-only` — unverified, NEVER `unsupported`
  (R-58d): a false narrowing is as wrong as a false widening.

### src/adapters/stringify-truncated.ts (new, post-M1 polish, cheap-fix backlog item 5)

`stringifyTruncated(value, maxLen = 500)` — `JSON.stringify`s an arbitrary value for inclusion in an
`Error` message, bounding it to `maxLen` characters and appending `…[truncated]` ONLY when actual
truncation happened (a normal, small payload's error message is unchanged). Extracted as a small
shared helper (mirrors this directory's existing precedent of sharing near-identical small helpers
across sibling adapter files, e.g. `not-implemented-error.ts`/`dash-metrics.ts`) rather than
duplicating the same truncation logic independently in `rpc-evm`/`rpc-solana` — its only two
current consumers.

### src/adapters/platform-explorer/index.ts (new, task 003-5, R-10/R-11)

`createPlatformExplorerAdapter` — LIVE, keyless REST, the ONLY live Dash Platform data source in
M1 (`dash-platform`'s `isAvailable()` is unconditionally `false`, F-3). Serves all seven Dash
capabilities: the same five `dash-platform` declares (`privacy.shielded_pool`,
`platform.identities/contracts/documents/credits`) PLUS its own two `*.history` methods (R-10),
which no other M1 adapter besides `pg-history` serves. One `ENDPOINT_BY_CAPABILITY` map dispatches
each capability to its REST path — confirmed by BOTH a live probe of
`platform-explorer.pshenmic.dev` AND by reading `packages/api/src/routes.js` in the
`pshenmic/platform-explorer` GitHub repo at its `master` HEAD (2026-07-22, not guessed):
`/status` (identities/data-contracts/documents/credits counts, ONE call answers all four
`platform.*` capabilities), `/transactions/shielded/statistic` (current shielded pool balance),
`/transactions/shield/history` and `/identities/history` (the two confirmed-live history series
endpoints). `normalize()` returns a single `Snapshot` for the five current-state capabilities and
a `Snapshot[]` for the two history ones, using the shared `DASH_METRIC`/`DASH_PLATFORM_ASSET`
vocabulary (`dash-metrics.ts`) so its output is metric-id-compatible with both `dash-platform`'s
own `normalize()` output and what `pg-history` reads back from `onchain.snapshots`.
**Documented implementation choice** (developer-guidelines §1.6, confirmed by the same live route
inspection): `platform-explorer` has NO "shielded pool balance over time" endpoint as of 2026-07 —
only a per-transition shield-AMOUNT history series exists (`/transactions/shield/history`) — so
`privacy.shielded_pool.history` uses that shield-inflow series as its representative time series,
under a DISTINCT metric id (`shielded_pool_shield_amount`, not `shielded_pool_balance_credits`) to
avoid implying it's the same quantity as the current-state balance. `platform.metrics.history`
reuses `/identities/history` (the `identities_total` metric, genuinely the same series over
time). `isAvailable()` unconditionally `{ok: true}` (keyless); `costOf()` `{credits: 0}`.

### src/adapters/dash-platform/index.ts (new, task 003-5, R-9, F-3)

`createDashPlatformAdapter` — interface + fixture-contract only in M1 (ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2/§11
decision, unchanged from v2.1): `capabilities()` declares the full five-capability DAPI-native
surface; the HTTP/gRPC step is a stub that throws `NotImplementedInM1Error` (never actually
reached through `CapabilityRegistry`, since `isAvailable()` below always skips this adapter
first); `normalize()` IS implemented and golden-tested against a **hand-built** fixture
(`test/fixtures/dash-platform/current-state.json`, explicitly marked as hand-built in its own
`.evidence.md` — there is no live gRPC transport in M1 to record from). The fixture's shape
mirrors the two DAPI RPC methods ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2's decision text names explicitly —
`getShieldedPoolState` (`poolBalance` + `metadata.height`) and `getTotalCreditsInPlatform`
(`credits` + `metadata.height`) — sourced from
`docs/onchain-analytics/raw/providers-addendum-2026-07-20.json`; the other three `platform.*`
capabilities reuse simple top-level count fields (`identitiesCount`/`dataContractsCount`/
`documentsCount`, matching `platform-explorer`'s own `/status` shape) since those three are not
named RPCs in the addendum, and this adapter's own HTTP step being unreachable in M1 regardless
means no real DAPI wire shape is being contradicted by that simplification. `isAvailable()` is
**UNCONDITIONALLY** `{ok: false, reason: 'dash-platform live transport deferred — see backlog,
use platform-explorer'}` — not "if the evonode is unreachable" — so `CapabilityRegistry` always
routes these five capabilities to `platform-explorer` instead, a real continuously-exercised
fallback path (R-11), proven by `test/registry.fallback.test.ts`. **No `@grpc/*` import anywhere
in this module** (verified by this task's acceptance grep) — a live gRPC transport is a separate,
non-blocking backlog item (ARCHITECTURE.md §11), not part of this task.

### src/adapters/dune/index.ts (new, task 003-5, R-8 — amended scope, architecture review cycle 1)

`createDuneAdapter` (no constructor deps — nothing to inject, there's no HTTP step to fake) —
interface/config-stub only: `capabilities()` declares `token.holders` on ethereum (the one
capability none of the other eight adapters covers); the HTTP step and `normalize()` both throw
`NotImplementedInM1Error` immediately (unreachable through `CapabilityRegistry`, same reasoning as
`dash-platform`); `isAvailable()` is unconditionally `{ok: false, reason: 'dune query authoring
deferred to M2'}`, **independent of `DUNE_API_KEY`'s presence** — live query authoring (query id,
parameterization) is deferred to M2 alongside `onchain_token_risk`, `token.holders`'s first real
tool consumer. No fixture, no golden/contract test beyond `test/dune.test.ts`'s four
registration/availability unit checks (task-scoped narrowing of R-8's original "contract-test on
a fixture" wording, accepted by the architecture reviewer, ARCHITECTURE.md §11) — none of the
four M1 Must tools depends on `token.holders`, so an empty `.env` stays fully functional (UC-1)
regardless of this adapter's state.

### src/pg/read-client.ts (new, task 003-5, R-12)

`createReadClient(deps?)` — a lazy, read-only Postgres client, used ONLY by
`adapters/pg-history/index.ts` (not a separate side-channel, F-2). `PgPoolLike`/`PgPoolCtor` are
a deliberately NARROW structural interface (just the one `query(text, values?)` method this module
actually calls) rather than importing `pg`'s full `Pool` type surface — this is what lets tests
inject a lightweight fake pool constructor that never opens a real socket (R-21). The real
`pg.Pool` is the production default, assigned via an explicit `as unknown as PgPoolCtor` cast at
the DI boundary (documented in-line: `Pool`'s actual `PoolConfig` constructor parameter is broader
than the narrow `{connectionString, options?}` shape this module ever passes it — the cast
documents that intentional narrowing rather than fighting structural variance). Behavior:

- **Lazy**: the underlying `Pool` is constructed on the FIRST `query()` call only — never at
  `createReadClient()`'s own call time, never at module load — and only once (a module-closure
  `pool` variable, reused across subsequent calls).
- **DSN never logged**: `isAvailable()` reports `{ok: false, reason: 'needs ONCHAIN_PG_URL'}`
  when `env.ONCHAIN_PG_URL` is absent — the reason string never contains the DSN value itself
  (D10/§7.2).
- **`search_path=onchain`** is set via the connection's own `options: '-c search_path=onchain'`
  (DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT's Postgres schema-isolation convention — literal string present in source,
  the task's acceptance grep finds it verbatim).
- **SELECT-only, enforced twice**: a runtime regex guard (`/^\s*select\b/i`) rejects any
  non-`SELECT` statement BEFORE it ever reaches the pool — defense in depth alongside the
  code-review grep gate (R-27) this task's acceptance also runs; this module never issues a write
  statement regardless of the guard.

**Pool hardening (adversarial cycle 1, fix D — 2 findings, both against the underlying `pg.Pool`,
never exercised with a live PG connection, R-21):**

1. **`pool.on('error', ...)` attached immediately after `new PoolCtor(...)` (D1):** an IDLE
   connection in the pool erroring out is a `pg.Pool`-documented event, fired independently of any
   in-flight `query()` call — without this listener, Node treats an unhandled `'error'` event on an
   `EventEmitter` as an UNCAUGHT EXCEPTION and crashes the whole process, exactly what this
   optional, best-effort history source must never do. The handler only writes to stderr
   (`pg/read-client: idle pool error (connection details never logged): <message>`) and otherwise
   ignores it. `PgPoolLike.on` is OPTIONAL on the interface (`on?(...)`) — only the real `pg.Pool`
   and any fake that chooses to model it need to implement it; `read-client.ts` calls it as
   `pool.on?.('error', ...)`. Also new: `PgPoolCtor`'s config type gained
   `connectionTimeoutMillis?`/`max?`, and `createReadClient` now ALWAYS passes them explicitly
   (`DEFAULT_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000`, `DEFAULT_MAX_POOL_SIZE = 3`) — conservative sizing for
   an optional, single-adapter, read-only client, never left at `pg`'s own looser defaults.
2. **Sanitized query-failure messages (D2):** any failure from the actual `pool.query(...)` call
   (as opposed to the guard clauses above, which already throw their own safe, DSN-free messages)
   is now caught, logged to stderr with its FULL detail (which may embed the DSN's host/port/user,
   e.g. `pg`'s own "connection to server at ... failed" text), then rethrown as the single
   sanitized message `'pg-history: database unavailable'` (`SANITIZED_QUERY_FAILURE_MESSAGE`) —
   with `{ cause: error }` attached (satisfies this repo's `preserve-caught-error` ESLint rule and
   preserves the chain for local debugging; never read by anything in this codebase's own error
   handling, which only ever reads `.message`). The DSN's host/port/user never reach the caller —
   and, transitively, never reach an MCP client.

`test/pg-history.contract.test.ts` gained a `describe('pool hardening ...')` block (3 new tests,
plus the pre-existing "fetch() lazily constructs the pool" test's exact-equality assertion was
updated to include `connectionTimeoutMillis`/`max`): a `FakePool.emitError(...)` helper (a small
in-test `EventEmitter`-shaped extension of the existing mock, tracking registered `'error'`
listeners) proves an idle-connection error is logged and never throws; a second test asserts the
pool constructor call includes `connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000, max: 3`; a third injects a
`FailingQueryPool` (deliberately WITHOUT an `on()` method, proving `read-client.ts` never assumes
it's present) whose `query()` always throws a DSN-shaped raw error, asserting the caller only ever
sees `'pg-history: database unavailable'` while the raw text reaches stderr only.

**Pool-construction hardening (post-M1 polish, cheap-fix backlog item 3):** `new PoolCtor(...)`
itself — a SYNCHRONOUS constructor call — sat OUTSIDE the D2 query-failure try/catch above, so a
throw from it (e.g. `pg` rejecting a malformed connection string) used to propagate its raw
message, potentially embedding the DSN's host/port/user, straight to the caller. Now wrapped in the
SAME sanitize-and-rethrow pattern as D2: the raw detail is logged to stderr only, and the caller
only ever sees the single sanitized `SANITIZED_QUERY_FAILURE_MESSAGE`, with `{ cause: error }`
attached (identical rationale to D2 — never read by this codebase's own error handling). One new
`test/pg-history.contract.test.ts` case: a `ThrowingPoolCtor` whose constructor throws a
DSN-bearing message — asserts the caller-visible error never contains the DSN/host fragment while
stderr does.

### src/adapters/pg-history/index.ts (new, task 003-5, R-12, F-2)

`createPgHistoryAdapter(deps?)` — a normal `ProviderAdapter` over `pg/read-client.ts` (NOT a
side-channel): `capabilities()` declares `privacy.shielded_pool.history` +
`platform.metrics.history` (the second-priority source behind `platform-explorer` in
`providers.config.ts`'s routes — used only when `ONCHAIN_PG_URL` is configured). `fetch()` issues
exactly one `SELECT ts, asset, metric, value_raw, value_num, source, height FROM snapshots WHERE
asset = $1 AND metric = ANY($2) ORDER BY ts DESC LIMIT $3` per capability, scoped to
`DASH_PLATFORM_ASSET` and the `METRICS_BY_CAPABILITY` list for that capability (the SAME metric
ids `platform-explorer`/`dash-platform`/the n8n snapshotter write under, from `dash-metrics.ts` —
so `pg-history` reads the vocabulary those sources actually wrote, not a separately-invented one).
`normalize()` converts each raw row into a canonical `Snapshot`, defensively parsing `ts`/`height`/
`value_num` from either a JS number OR a string (`toFiniteNumber` helper) — real Postgres `BIGINT`
columns (the `ts_bucket`/`ts`/`height`/`created_at` columns per DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT §1, Postgres
stage) come back from `node-postgres` as STRINGS by default (its own precision-preserving
behavior for the `int8` OID), not JS numbers; not handling that would have silently produced
`NaN`/string-typed `ts` fields. `isAvailable()` delegates to the read client's own DSN check
(deps: `readClient?`/`poolCtor?`/`env?` — all injectable, same DI convention as the rest of this
package) and never surfaces the DSN value itself.

`test/pg-history.contract.test.ts` (R-12, F-2, mocked pg client — NEVER a live database
connection, R-21) — 11 tests: `isAvailable()` false-without-DSN (exact reason string, no crash)
and true-with-DSN (asserted to never contain the injected fake DSN via `JSON.stringify`);
`capabilities()`/`costOf()`; a fake `PgPoolCtor` (`FakePool`, tracking every constructor call +
its own `queryCalls`) injected all the way through `pg-history`'s `poolCtor` dependency into
`read-client.ts`'s REAL lazy-construction/`search_path` logic — proving BOTH modules' behavior
from one file, per the task's own naming ("mocked pg client", not "mocked read-client"): lazy
construction (zero instances before the first `fetch()` call), pool reuse across multiple `fetch()`
calls (still exactly one instance), the exact SQL text (`SELECT`-only, no
`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`) and bound parameters, and `normalize()`'s stringified-bigint-to-number
conversion; an unsupported-capability rejection asserted to never leak the fake DSN into its
error message; and two direct `read-client.ts` checks (non-`SELECT` statement rejected before
ever touching the pool; missing-DSN rejected without ever constructing a pool).

Six real live fixtures + one hand-built fixture, each with a committed `.evidence.md`:

line, "HAND-BUILT, NOT RECORDED LIVE") — there is no live gRPC transport to record from in M1

for internal consistency, even though the file itself isn't a live capture of `dash-platform`.

### scripts/record-fixture.mjs (new, task 003-4, R-22; extended task 003-5)

Manual dev CLI (plain Node ESM, **not** part of CI — nothing in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
invokes it, and no test imports it, both verified by this task's own acceptance greps), imported
from the **built** `dist/index.js` (mirrors `packages/mcp-server/scripts/smoke-dist.mjs`'s own
"operate on `dist/`, fail fast if missing" precedent; requires
`pnpm --filter @onchain-intel/core build` first). Usage:
`node packages/core/scripts/record-fixture.mjs <coingecko|dexscreener|defillama|rpc-evm|
rpc-solana|platform-explorer> <chain> [address|protocolSlug]`.

For `coingecko`/`dexscreener`/`defillama`/`rpc-evm`/`rpc-solana`: performs exactly ONE real live
HTTP call per invocation, through the target adapter's own real HTTP step. Writes the call's raw
result (whatever shape that adapter's own fetch step returns — its private `{chain, [limit,] raw}`
envelope, not just the vendor wire body) to `test/fixtures/<adapter>/<name>.json`, plus a sibling
`<name>.evidence.md` recording `recorded_at` (ISO timestamp), `endpoint` (the exact URL touched),
`http_status`, `capability`, `args`, and two field-list lines: `envelope_fields` (this adapter's
own hand-off shape's keys) and `vendor_response_fields` (the actual **vendor** response body's own
top-level keys — the field-list evidence R-22 cares about, for vendor-drift monitoring). The
endpoint/status are captured via an **instrumented `fetchImpl`** injected into the adapter factory
(wraps the global HTTP client, records `{url, status}` as a side effect of the SAME one call) —
never a second, separate probe request. On a live-call failure, no fixture JSON is written; the
evidence file instead records the error and an explicit note that a hand-authored minimal fixture
must be created and clearly marked as such before the corresponding contract test can pass (all
ten live calls across both tasks succeeded — see the fixtures sections above — so no
hand-authored substitute was needed for any of them, except `dash-platform`, which this script
never records at all — see its own section above).

**`platform-explorer` is a deliberate exception to "one invocation, one call"** (task 003-5): ONE
invocation performs FOUR real live calls, one per distinct underlying REST endpoint this adapter's
seven capabilities collapse onto (`/status` alone serves four of them, so it's called once, not
four times) — matching the task file's own single documented command line
(`record-fixture.mjs platform-explorer dash # live + history эндпоинты`), which intentionally asks
for both current-state and history endpoints together rather than four separate invocations. Each
of the four calls writes its own `test/fixtures/platform-explorer/<name>.{json,evidence.md}` pair,
in the exact same evidence format as the single-call path (via a shared `recordOneCall()` helper —
extracted so the two paths share evidence-writing logic instead of duplicating it); a failure in
one of the four does not abort the other three (each is attempted, `process.exitCode` is set to 1
if any failed).

### test/fixtures/coingecko/, test/fixtures/dexscreener/, test/fixtures/defillama/ (new, task 003-4, R-21/R-22)

Six real fixtures, one per `(adapter, chain)` pair (`defillama`'s two are named by protocol slug
instead — `uniswap.json`/`raydium.json`), each with a committed `.evidence.md`, all recorded
2026-07-22 by real live calls (no hand-authored substitutes were needed — every call returned
`HTTP 200`): `coingecko/ethereum.json` (USDC on Ethereum), `coingecko/solana.json` (USDC on
Solana), `dexscreener/ethereum.json` (`search?q=ETH`, 2 of 30 returned pairs are actually
`chainId: "ethereum"` — the rest are noise from other chains, which `normalize()`'s filter is
exactly what's being golden-tested), `dexscreener/solana.json` (`search?q=SOL`, 15 of 30 matching
`chainId: "solana"`), `defillama/uniswap.json` / `defillama/raydium.json` (full multi-chain TVL
payloads). Committed as-is (Prettier-formatted, same JSON semantics) — these are the actual bytes
a real 2026-07-22 API response produced, not hand-edited.

**`defillama/*.json` series trimmed post-recording (adversarial cycle 2, fixture-size finding
6):** the two DeFiLlama fixtures were originally ~3.6MB (`uniswap.json`) and ~568KB
(`raydium.json`) — every `chainTvls[*]` entry (49 chains for uniswap, 5 for raydium) plus the
top-level entry each carry a FULL daily `tvl` time series going back years. A one-off `node -e`
script (not committed — trivial, run-once) sliced every one of those `tvl` arrays down to their
LAST 3 points each, then re-formatted with Prettier to match this directory's existing convention.
**Zero test-coverage loss:** `defillama.normalize()`'s own `lastTotalLiquidityUsd()` only ever
reads `series[series.length - 1]` — the golden tests derive their expected values FROM the
(now-trimmed) fixture dynamically, never a hardcoded magic number, so they still pass unchanged.
All non-series fields (`name`, `chains`, `currentChainTvls`, `symbol`, `address`, etc.) are
untouched, byte-for-byte from the original 2026-07-22 capture — only the two named series' array
LENGTH changed, never any individual point's own values. Post-trim: `uniswap.json` ~24KB,
`raydium.json` ~4KB. Both `.evidence.md` files gained a `series trimmed to last 3 points
post-recording (fixture-size finding, cycle 2): ...` note recording exactly what was trimmed and why.

### test/coingecko.contract.test.ts, test/dexscreener.contract.test.ts, test/defillama.contract.test.ts (new, task 003-4, R-5/R-6/R-7)

16 tests total (5 + 6 + 5), all offline (no network — the R-21 acceptance grep confirms no test
file references the fixture-recording script or a raw fetch client). Each file: loads its
committed fixture(s) via plain `readFileSync`+`JSON.parse` (no import of the recording script);
calls `adapter.normalize(cap, fixture)` directly and asserts an **exact** expected canonical
object — deterministic fields (chain/address/symbol/name/source/id/dexId/...) as literal
constants, `fetchedAt` via an injected fixed `now()` (so it's exactly assertable instead of racing
the real wall clock), and inherently-volatile vendor numbers (price/marketCap/liquidity/volume/tvl)
re-derived from the SAME already-loaded fixture object (still fully offline/deterministic — the
fixture is frozen) rather than hand-copied as magic numbers that would go stale. Each file also
asserts `capabilities()`/`costOf()`/`isAvailable()`, and one `fetch()`-level test per adapter using
an **injected fake `fetchImpl`** (returns a canned `Response` built from the same fixture's
`raw`, no real HTTP) asserting the exact documented endpoint URL was built — regression coverage
for the HTTP-step logic itself, following the same injected-fake-`fetchImpl` pattern
`safe-fetch.test.ts` already established (task 003-2). `dexscreener.contract.test.ts` additionally
asserts the recorded shape directly (`Array.isArray(fixture.raw) === false`, `fixture.raw.pairs`
is an array) — a standing regression test for the shape-trap this task's live probe resolved.

— plus, added task 003-4: `createCoingeckoAdapter`/`CoingeckoAdapterDeps`,
`createDexscreenerAdapter`/`DexscreenerAdapterDeps`, `createDefillamaAdapter`/

(003-5): `createRpcEvmAdapter`/`RpcEvmAdapterDeps`, `createRpcSolanaAdapter`/
`RpcSolanaAdapterDeps`, `createPlatformExplorerAdapter`/`PlatformExplorerAdapterDeps`,

directly). This is the only import path `mcp-server` (or any future consumer, including

(`CacheStore`/`PassthroughCacheStore`/`SsrfBlockedError`/`CapabilityUnavailableError`/

internally, or by this package's own tests/scripts, via their concrete module paths; nothing outside
this package needs them directly until a future task gives a concrete reason to widen the public
surface further.)

**`ttlFor` moved OUT of that list in WI-28**, and the sentence above is why it is worth recording
rather than silently editing: the concrete reason arrived. Five tables in three documents (both
READMEs' tool tables, both READMEs' caching tables, and `docs/architectures/system-architecture.md`
§3.2 — the one `cache/ttl.ts` names as its source) restate these seconds, and
`mcp-server/test/readme-tool-table.test.ts` checks each against the code. A gate cannot verify a
restatement it has no way to reach. `NEGATIVE_TTL_SECONDS` was exported alongside it in the first
draft and taken back out in the same session: no document restates it and no gate reads it, so its
justification was borrowed from its neighbour rather than earned.

### test/chain-address.test.ts

16 tests (Phase 2, R-1/R-2 support, ARCHITECTURE.md §4.1 contract): four official EIP-55 test
vectors (https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-55) exercised with `it.each` — each vector's checksum
form, its lowercased form, and an aggressively re-cased ("shouted" body, `0x` prefix restored)
form all normalize to the identical checksummed output (the case-independence contract);
`isValidAddress` accepts all three casings; a missing `0x` prefix is accepted equivalently to a
prefixed one; too-short / too-long / non-hex inputs are rejected by both functions (`isValidAddress
→ false`, `normalizeAddress → throws`). Solana: the well-known System Program ID
(`11111...1` × 32, all-zero pubkey) and the SPL Token Program ID are both accepted and returned
byte-for-byte unchanged (proving no case-folding, unlike EVM); a truncated address (wrong decoded
length) and a string containing base58-excluded characters (`0`, `O`, `I`, `l`) are both rejected.
Dash: `isValidAddress('dash', *)` is always `false` (including on an empty string) and
`normalizeAddress('dash', *)` always throws — the documented "not validated" contract, made
concrete and regression-tested.

### test/types.test.ts

19 tests (Phase 2, closes R-1/R-2): every schema (`ChainSchema`, `TokenSchema`, `BalanceSchema`,
`WalletSchema`, `PoolSchema`, `OhlcvSchema`, `SnapshotSchema`) validates one realistic example and
rejects that same example with an added unknown field (the `.strict()` contract). Additional
coverage: `TokenSchema` accepts all-optional-fields-omitted input; `BalanceSchema` and
`SnapshotSchema` each get a dedicated test asserting a `>2^53` integer string
(`amountRaw`/`valueRaw`) survives `.parse()` unchanged and `typeof === 'string'` (DB-SCHEMA §1.7 —
the exact reason these fields are strings, not numbers, made concrete rather than just asserted in
a docstring); `SnapshotSchema` additionally gets a JSON-round-trip test (`JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))`
re-validates identically) — this is the "unit test on serialization" R-2 acceptance criterion.

### test/registry.test.ts, safe-fetch.test.ts, rate-limit.test.ts, args-hash.test.ts (task 003-2)

38 tests total across the four files: `registry.test.ts` (13) — routing by (capability, chain),
`isAvailable()===false` → skip-to-next (the dash-platform→platform-explorer semantic), fetch/normalize
error → next adapter, all-failed → typed error, cache-seam interaction via an injected fake
CacheStore; `safe-fetch.test.ts` (9) — allowlist rejection BEFORE any network touch (injected fetch
impl records calls), redirect re-checks per hop, max-3-hops cap; `rate-limit.test.ts` (6) — token
bucket per provider with injected clock (no real timers); `args-hash.test.ts` (10) — canonical
key-ordered serialization (insertion-order independence; nested objects). Note: deriveArgsHash
deliberately does NOT normalize addresses itself — that is the caller's job per §7.2 — and its
signature (capability + args only) is what keeps env material out of the derivation.

### test/cache.test.ts, cache-stats.test.ts (task 003-3, R-13/R-14/R-15)

30 + 5 = 35 tests total across the two files, all against real `mkdtempSync` temp-dir sqlite files
or `:memory:`-equivalent fakes — **never** the real `DATA_DIR`/`~/.onchain-intel` (verified: `git
status` and `ls ~/.onchain-intel` both stay clean/absent after a full `pnpm test` run).

- **`cache.test.ts`** (30): `data-dir` (env override, homedir default, `cacheDbPath` join) —
  closes the R-13 "not cwd-relative" acceptance directly, not just via the source-grep.
  `ttlFor` — one `it.each` row per ARCHITECTURE.md §3.2 table capability + the documented
  fallback-default case. `SqliteCacheStore` — bootstraps exactly the 9 real `adapterRegistrations`
  into `providers` (queried via a **second, independent** raw `better-sqlite3` connection to the
  same file, proving the data is actually persisted, not just held in the first connection's
  cache); miss-before-any-write; set-then-get hit with `ageMs`; **the upsert test** writes twice
  with a 5ms real gap, then asserts via a raw connection that the row **count stays 1** (not 2 —
  proves upsert, not append) **and** that `value_json`/`created_at`/`expires_at` all actually
  changed to the second write's values (proves `DO UPDATE`, not a silent `DO NOTHING` that would
  pin the first, now-stale, value); **the TTL-expiry test** force-expires a row via a second raw
  connection directly setting `expires_at` into the past (deliberately not waiting out
  `token.price`'s real 60s TTL) and asserts both a miss AND that the row count drops to 0 (deleted
  on read); **the FK test** asserts `store.set()` for an unregistered provider id
  **rejects** — proving `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` is genuinely active on the store's own connection,
  not merely present as a source string (that's what the task's separate grep checks); **the
  args-order test** derives two hashes from differently-key-ordered args via 003-2's
  `deriveArgsHash`, asserts they're equal, and proves a `set()` under one order is read back by a
  `get()` under the other. `LruHotLayer` — miss-before-set, hit-within-ttl, **real expiry** (a
  short real ttl, e.g. 20ms, plus a short real `setTimeout` wait, e.g. 80ms — chosen specifically to
  avoid waiting out any capability's real multi-second-to-hour TTL), and the `ttlMs <= 0` /
  not-stored guard. `TwoLevelStore` — against a hand-written `FakeCacheStore` test double (proves
  the orchestration logic in isolation, no timing dependency): hot-miss → persistent consulted
  exactly once; `set()` writes both layers and a subsequent `get()` is served hot without
  re-touching persistent; a **pre-seeded persistent-only** entry is promoted into hot on first
  `get()` and served from hot (persistent call count stays at 1) on the second. `createCacheStore`
  — one assembly-level smoke test (real `SqliteCacheStore` + real `LruHotLayer` via the factory,
  round-tripping a value).
- **`cache-stats.test.ts`** (5): `getCacheStats()` starts empty; `recordCacheAccess` increments the
  right per-capability bucket; a `vi.spyOn(process.stdout, 'write')` / `process.stderr, 'write')`
  pair proves the line goes to stderr only (never stdout — M0 §7.3) and contains
  `cache=hit`/`provider=`/`capability=`/`ageMs=` in the exact documented format; a miss logs
  `ageMs=0`; and the R-15 acceptance scenario itself — `TwoLevelStore` against a `FakeCacheStore`,
  first lookup miss then a hit after `set()`, both increments visible via `getCacheStats()`
  (`_meta.cache`'s own E2E wiring is task 003-7's scope, per the task file — not re-tested here).

### Post-M1 fix (2026-07-23): CoinGecko Pro auth contour — supersedes the demo-only wording above

The `coingecko` adapter section above ("`COINGECKO_API_KEY` … sent as the `x-cg-demo-api-key`
header") describes the original M1 state and is superseded: CoinGecko's Pro subscription is a
SEPARATE auth contour — host `pro-api.coingecko.com` + `x-cg-pro-api-key` header — and the pro
host ignores the demo header entirely (live-probed 2026-07-23: `x-cg-demo-api-key` on the pro
host still yields `10002 API Key Missing`), so a Pro key routed through the demo path FAILS
rather than merely losing rate-limit headroom. `createCoingeckoAdapter` now reads
`COINGECKO_PRO_API_KEY` as well: when set, the URL is built against the pro host with the pro
header (precedence over the demo key when both are set); the contour is declared by WHICH env
var is set — never sniffed from key format, since both tiers issue `CG-…`-shaped keys. Both
hosts were already in this adapter's `adapterRegistrations` allowlist (unchanged);
`isAvailable()`/`costOf()` unchanged (keyless tier still works, still 0 credits).
`coingecko.contract.test.ts` +3 (`it.each` over the three contours: demo → free host + demo
header; pro → pro host + pro header; both → pro wins), and the existing keyless `fetch()` test
now also asserts NO auth header of either contour is sent — core total **212**.

### M2 begins — task 005-1 (R-29/R-30/R-31/R-32/R-33): three new canonical types + the tenth adapter (`nansen`, registration/skeleton only) — first PAID adapter of this package

> **Recovery notice (2026-07-24, task 005-7):** this section and the two that follow it

> (the whole file was overwritten with a placeholder for a moment). The base M0/M1 content above

.AGENTS.md`); the task 005-4/005-5/005-7 sections below this one were recovered byte-exact from
> this agent's own conversation transcript (each had been read back verbatim earlier in the same
> session). **These three sections (005-1/005-2/005-3) could NOT be recovered verbatim** — neither
> git (never committed) nor the transcript (never read in full) had a copy. What follows is a
> **reconstruction**, written by re-inspecting the actual current source/test files on disk (which
> were untouched by the mistake) rather than by recalling the original prose — facts (file names,
> exact test counts, schema shapes, DDL) are re-verified against the real files as of 2026-07-24;
> the ORIGINAL narrative wording, and any incidental in-review detail that left no trace in the
> code itself, is lost. Treat this reconstruction as accurate on facts, not as a byte-exact
> restoration.

Three new canonical zod types (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.1 D5-extension) + the package's tenth
`providers.config.ts` adapter registration, `nansen` — the first PAID adapter this package has ever
declared (D6, ADR-001).

**New files:**

- `src/types/entity-label.ts` — `EntityLabelSchema` (37 lines): `chain`/`address` BOTH optional
  (the only M2 type where that's true — a Nansen `EntitySearchResult` can be a cross-chain entity
  with neither), `tags[]`/`labels[]` defaulting to `[]` (an empty array is a VALID result, "no
  labels found", not an error — R-32's own acceptance criterion), plus the standard
  `source`/`fetchedAt` envelope fields every M1/M2 canonical type carries.
- `src/types/smart-money-flow.ts` — `SmartMoneyFlowSchema` (55 lines): `chain`/`tokenAddress`/
  `tokenSymbol`, four `netflow{1h,24h,7d,30d}Usd` numbers, optional `traderCount`/`tokenAgeDays`/
  `tokenSectors`, and `topHolders[]` — a deliberate SUBSET of Nansen's `TGMHolder` DTO (anti-
  corruption layer, D4): only `address`/`addressLabel`/`tokenAmount`/`valueUsd`/
  `ownershipPercentage` are kept, `total_outflow`/`total_inflow`/`balance_change_*` are dropped.
- `src/types/token-risk-score.ts` — `TokenRiskScoreSchema` (50 lines): `chain`/`address`, optional
  `marketCapUsd`/`marketCapGroup`/`isStablecoin`, and two SEPARATE indicator arrays
  (`riskIndicators`/`rewardIndicators`, R-33 "not flattened") sharing one element shape
  (`indicatorType`, optional `score`/`signal`/`signalPercentile`/`lastTriggerOn`) — `signal`/
  `signalPercentile` are plain JS `number`, not the wei-like `TEXT` pattern (DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT
  §1.7 doesn't apply — these aren't on-chain integers, R-33).
- `src/adapters/nansen/index.ts`, `.AGENTS.md` (bootstrap, new directory) — `createNansenAdapter(deps?:
NansenAdapterDeps): ProviderAdapter`, this task's own skeleton: `capabilities()` returns the three
  new routes (`smart-money.flows`/`entity.labels`/`token.risk`, chains `['ethereum', 'solana']` —
  the same subset every M1 tool already used, OQ-3); `isAvailable()` reads `deps.env ?? process.env`
  INSIDE the method body itself (never at module load, never logged, never folded into a cache key)
  and returns `{ok: true}` only when `NANSEN_API_KEY` is a non-empty string; `fetch()`/`normalize()`
  are STUBS at this point (real from task 005-4).
- `src/adapters/nansen/account-state.ts` — `NansenAccountState`/`createNansenAccountState()`: a
  factory (never a module singleton, ARCHITECTURE.md §8) holding the live `{plan, credits_remaining,
usageAtObserve, observedAtMs, dayBucketMs}` snapshot the later budget gate resyncs against, plus
  `markUnreconciled()`/`isUnreconciled()`/`clearUnreconciled()` (R-38, wired task 005-5) and
  `hasWarned()`/`markWarned()` (the budget-warning-threshold flag, wired task 005-3).
- `packages/core/scripts/generate-nansen-cost-table.mjs` — generator script (later consumed by task
  005-3's `cost-table.ts`), reading `docs/onchain-analytics/raw/nansen-openapi-2026-07-23.json`'s
  per-operation `x-credit-cost` extensions into a committed, Prettier-formatted literal.

**Edits:**

- `src/providers.config.ts` — `nansen` becomes the 10th `adapterRegistrations` entry
  (`hosts: ['api.nansen.ai']`, `rateLimit: {capacity: 5, refillPerSec: 1}`,
  `requiresEnv: ['NANSEN_API_KEY']`) and gains 3 new routes (`smart-money.flows`/`entity.labels`/
  `token.risk`, `adapterIds: ['nansen']`, **no fallback** — none of these three capabilities has a
  free equivalent, unlike every M1 route).
- `src/index.ts` — re-exports `createNansenAdapter`/`NansenAdapterDeps` (the package's ONLY publicly
  exported factory for this adapter — no separate raw-client export) plus the three new schema/type
  pairs.

**`registry.ts` — ZERO changes** (the hard constraint every subsequent M2 task re-verifies).

Verification (re-derived, task 005-7): `nansen.types.test.ts` — **14** tests (schema validation for
all three new types, `.strict()` unknown-field rejection, the optional-`chain`/`address` contract on
`EntityLabelSchema`); `nansen.adapter.test.ts` — registration + `isAvailable()` + registry-
integration coverage (11 tests as of the current, post-005-4 state — task 005-4 later replaced one
stub-message assertion, net count unchanged by that edit per that task's own section below).

### Task 005-2 (R-34/R-35): `usage` table + `BudgetStore` + `SqliteBudgetStore` — the provider-agnostic credit-budget ledger

The credit-budget ledger — provider-agnostic by design (the interface itself never mentions
`nansen`; only test files reference that provider id as a string).

**New files:**

- `src/cache/day-bucket.ts` — `dayBucketMs(ts: number): number` — `Math.floor(ts / 86_400_000) *
86_400_000`, the epoch-ms UTC start of the day `ts` falls in. A single shared export so the budget
  gate (task 005-3) and any future `_meta.budget`-surfacing tool handler compute the SAME bucket
  boundary for the SAME instant — pure UTC arithmetic, no local-timezone/DST logic, no `Date`
  object (DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT §1.2), mirroring the n8n snapshotter's own `ts_bucket =
floor(ts/3600000)*3600000` pattern (CLAUDE.n8n.md) at a day-width instead of an hour-width.
- `src/cache/budget-store.ts` — `BudgetStore` interface (`checkAndReserve`/`recordDelta`/
  `getUsage`, all `Promise`-returning for interface parity with `CacheStore` and forward-compat with
  a future Postgres implementation) + `SqliteBudgetStore`/`createBudgetStore()`. Opens its OWN
  connection to the SAME cache file `SqliteCacheStore` uses (`cacheDbPath()`), self-sufficient
  bootstrap (`db.exec(CACHE_DDL)` idempotently, upserts `providers` rows with
  `kind = registration.tier` before any `usage` FK write can reference one — task 012-3; it wrote
  the literal `'unknown'` under `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` until then, to avoid replicating
  `SqliteCacheStore`'s hardcoded id list, and now derives the same value from the same field as
  that sibling under `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE SET kind = excluded.kind`).
  `checkAndReserve` uses `db.transaction(fn).immediate()` (WAL-specific — `IMMEDIATE`, not the
  driver's default `DEFERRED`, so a concurrent writer's read-then-upgrade race fails fast via the
  busy-handler instead of racing past it) to atomically compare `used + cost` against `ceiling` and,
  only if it fits, additively reserve `cost` via the SAME upsert SQL `recordDelta` uses (signed
  delta, `MAX(0, ...)`-clamped). `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` re-issued on every connection open
  (connection-scoped, DB-SCHEMA-CONCEPT §1.6).

**Edits:**

- `src/cache/ddl.ts` — new `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS usage (provider TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES
providers(id), day INTEGER NOT NULL, credits_used INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, updated_at INTEGER
NOT NULL, PRIMARY KEY (provider, day))` — filling in a forward-compat note this file had already
  reserved a place for. `credits_used` is a plain `INTEGER` (a small-in-range internal engine
  counter), deliberately NOT the `value_raw TEXT` exact-value pattern `cache_entries`/`Snapshot`
  use elsewhere (R-34) — additive-only, never a plain overwrite.
- `src/cache/sqlite-store.ts` — no schema change; `CACHE_DDL`'s single string now includes `usage`
  too, so `SqliteCacheStore`'s own idempotent `db.exec(CACHE_DDL)` bootstrap picks it up for free.
- `src/index.ts` — re-exports `BudgetStore`/`createBudgetStore`.

**`registry.ts` — ZERO changes.**

Verification (re-derived): `budget-store.test.ts` covers `checkAndReserve`'s atomicity/ceiling
behavior, `recordDelta`'s signed-delta accumulation with the `MAX(0, ...)` floor, `getUsage`'s
read-only contract, the self-sufficient `providers` bootstrap (a standalone `new
SqliteBudgetStore({dbPath: ':memory:'})` works without a co-constructed `SqliteCacheStore`), and
the `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` re-issue (an unregistered provider throws on write).

### Task 005-3 (R-36/R-37): cost-table codegen + `costOf()` + `NansenAccountState` + pre-call budget gate (`ensureBudget`, not yet wired into `fetch()`)

The pre-call budget gate itself — built and unit-tested in isolation here; genuinely WIRED into
`fetch()` only in task 005-5 (system-architecture.md §3.2's own phased-build note).

**New files:**

- `src/adapters/nansen/cost-table.ts` — **GENERATED, DO NOT EDIT BY HAND**
  (`scripts/generate-nansen-cost-table.mjs`, task 005-1's own script, run here for the first time):
  `NANSEN_COST_TABLE: Readonly<Record<string, {free: number; pro: number}>>` — exactly the 8
  `(method, path)` entries M2's three capabilities actually call (`GET /api/v1/account`,
  `POST /api/v1/smart-money/netflow`, `POST /api/v1/tgm/holders`, `POST /api/v1/search/general`,
  `POST /api/v1/search/entity-name`, `POST /api/v1/profiler/address/labels`,
  `POST /api/v1/tgm/indicators`, `POST /api/v1/tgm/token-information`) — a key absent from this
  table resolves to `Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY` at the caller, never `0` (R-37 MIN-3, fail-closed).
  Also exports `NANSEN_PREMIUM_LABELS_COST` (150cr flat surcharge for `/tgm/holders`'
  `premium_labels: true` variant, sourced from the SAME spec's `x-credit-cost-variants` extension).
- `src/adapters/nansen/cost-of.ts` — `costOf(accountState, cap, args): number` — sums
  `NANSEN_COST_TABLE` entries for the fixed `cap -> (method,path)[]` routing table under the LIVE
  plan (`accountState.get()?.plan ?? 'free'`, the conservative default before any resync). Fail-
  closed for an unrecognized capability OR a cost-table-absent endpoint: `Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY`.
- `src/adapters/nansen/budget-gate.ts` — `createNansenBudgetGate(deps).ensureBudget(cap, args)`:
  fixes `bucket = dayBucketMs(now())` once at entry; resyncs `GET /account` (`refreshAccount()`)
  ONLY when the current snapshot can't be trusted (cold start, a stale day-bucket, or an explicit
  `markUnreconciled()`); computes the exact price via `costOf()`; computes
  `effectiveCeilingFor(snapshot, dailyCreditCap)` — the anchor-rebased formula
  `usageAtObserve + creditsRemainingAtObserve`, THEN `min()` against the self-imposed cap
  (the naive `min(creditsRemainingAtObserve, cap)` alone double-counts already-spent credits on a
  mid-bucket resync — a real defect this formula fixes, exported and directly unit-tested via
  `effectiveCeilingFor()`); atomically reserves via `BudgetStore.checkAndReserve`; on success, emits
  an at-most-once-per-SNAPSHOT `nansen budget warn:` stderr line once usage crosses `budgetWarnRatio`
  (default 0.8) of the ceiling — **not** once per bucket: every successful resync clears the flag,
  and mid-bucket resyncs are routine (vdd-multi cycle 4, L-11). The cap is no longer merely
  "optional" — see the Q-2 / cycle-4 section at the end of this file. Auth header is the literal `apiKey: <NANSEN_API_KEY>` — NOT the
  generic Web credentials-header + token-prefix scheme some other Nansen surfaces use (a live-probed
  M-4 review finding: getting this wrong fails EVERY resync, a silent total lockout).

**Edits:**

- `src/adapters/nansen/account-state.ts` — unchanged in shape from task 005-1's stub; genuinely
  READ/WRITTEN for the first time by `budget-gate.ts` above.
- `src/index.ts` — no new public re-export **in this task** (the gate itself is a package-internal
  implementation seam, never re-exported — system-architecture.md §3.2 "Budget gate — не отдельный
  wrapper-объект"). Q-2 later added three PURE, inert re-exports from this module — `DAILY_CAP_OFF`,
  `deriveDailyCap`, `DailyCreditCapConfig` — for the mcp-server env layer; `createNansenBudgetGate`
  is still deliberately not exported.

**`registry.ts` — ZERO changes.**

**Deliberately NOT in this task:** `ensureBudget()` is NOT yet called from
`adapters/nansen/index.ts`'s `fetch()` (task 005-5's own scope) — this task only builds and
unit-tests the mechanism in isolation, against a directly-constructed gate, never through the full
adapter.

Verification (re-derived): `nansen.cost-of.test.ts` — **12** tests (per-capability pricing under
both plans, the `premium_labels` surcharge, fail-closed `Infinity` for an unknown capability/
endpoint); `nansen.budget-gate.test.ts` — **14** tests (`effectiveCeilingFor`'s anchor-rebased
formula proven against the naive-formula's own defect, the two refusal cases — 150cr against a
100cr balance, 100cr exhaustive escalation against a 95-of-100 remaining ceiling — resync-only-
when-needed, the warn-threshold at-most-once-per-bucket line, dayBucketMs bound at entry immune to
a mid-flight `now()` change).

### Task 005-4 (R-29/R-45): HTTP step + `normalize()` for all three M2 capabilities — spec-derived provisional fixtures, secret discipline proven by test

Test count rose from **277** (005-3's end state) to **295** — 18 new tests: `test/nansen.contract
.test.ts` (14, new — golden/contract R-31/R-32/R-33 + HTTP-contract TC-UNIT-06..12), `test/nansen
.secrets.test.ts` (3, new — R-45 TC-SEC-13..15), `test/nansen.adapter.test.ts` (net 0 — one
005-1-era stub-message assertion replaced by two cases matching the now-real `fetch()`/
`normalize()`, see "Edits" below). `packages/mcp-server` stayed at 75, untouched — workspace total
**352 → 370**. Stub-First: Phase 1 landed `endpoints.ts`'s seven request-builder signatures +
`normalize.ts`'s three normalization function signatures against the spec-derived fixtures,
`tsc --noEmit` clean. Phase 2 filled in the real HTTP call construction + normalization logic —
full 295-test suite green.

**New files:**

- `src/adapters/nansen/endpoints.ts` — one thin function per endpoint
  (`postSmartMoneyNetflow`/`postTgmHolders`/`postTgmIndicators`/`postTgmTokenInformation`/
  `postSearchGeneral`/`postSearchEntityName`/`postProfilerAddressLabels`), each returning
  `Promise<{body: unknown; creditsUsedHeader: string | null}>` — the credits header is handed UP
  unopened, parsed only by task 005-5's reconciliation, never by the endpoint itself. All funnel
  through one internal `callEndpoint()`: `throttle('nansen', RATE_LIMIT)` → `safeFetch(url, opts,
HOSTS, fetchImpl)` (`HOSTS` sourced from `adapterRegistrations`, never a literal) → status
  handling (`429`→`NansenRateLimitedError` naming `retry-after` in its message text, `402`→
  `NansenPaymentRequiredError`, other non-`ok`→plain `Error` with a `stringifyTruncated()`-bounded
  body) → `response.json()`. The literal `apiKey` header (not the generic Web credentials-header +
  token-prefix scheme some other Nansen surfaces use) — this file's own comment near the header
  construction deliberately avoids spelling out that OTHER scheme's two words, same convention
  `budget-gate.ts` already established (this whole directory is grepped for them in every task's
  acceptance). Request bodies are minimal (only the fields each capability needs; every vendor
  request schema is `additionalProperties: false`, confirmed via targeted `jq` lookups against the
  committed openapi spec, never the whole 630KB file read at once). `postSearchGeneral` passes
  `chain` as the vendor's own optional narrowing filter so `TokenSearchResult.chain` values stay
  consistent with the caller's narrow `NansenChain` — `normalize.ts` never has to reconcile an
  arbitrary vendor chain string against the strict canonical `ChainSchema` enum.
- `src/adapters/nansen/normalize.ts` — three pure functions (`normalizeSmartMoneyFlow`/
  `normalizeEntityLabels`/`normalizeTokenRiskScore`), each taking this module's own "raw hand-off"
  interface (`SmartMoneyFlowsFetchResult`/`EntityLabelsFetchResult`/`TokenRiskFetchResult` — never
  seen by `CapabilityRegistry`, anti-corruption layer D4) + an injectable `now()`, returning the
  canonical zod type via `Schema.parse(...)`. `normalizeEntityLabels` branches on which of
  `raw.profilerLabels`/`raw.search` is populated (set by `fetch()`'s own routing) — exhaustive tier
  maps `ProfilerAddressLabelsResponse.data[].label` into ONE entry's `labels[]`
  (`premiumRequested: true`); default/token-scoped tier maps `GeneralSearchResponse.{tokens[],
entities[]}` plus (when token-scoped) one entry per `TGMHoldersResponse.data[]` row
  (`premiumRequested: false`) — an empty resulting array, or an entry with `labels: []`, is a VALID
  result, never thrown (R-32). Two capabilities' hand-off shapes deliberately carry a sub-call whose
  response is fetched but NOT consumed by normalization: `entity.labels`' `entityName` (search/
  entity-name is always called alongside search/general per `cost-of.ts`'s own `endpointsFor()`,
  but `EntityNameSearchItem`'s bare `entity_name` strings don't map onto any `EntityLabel` field)
  and `token.risk`'s `tokenInformation` (data-model.md §4.1 traces every `TokenRiskScore` field to
  `TGMIndicatorsResponse` alone) — both are kept in the hand-off object anyway (not discarded in
  `fetch()`) for structural completeness / a future extension without a second live call, not
  speculative dead code (developer-guidelines §1.6 documented choice, spelled out in each
  interface's own docstring).
- `test/fixtures/nansen/{smart-money-netflow,tgm-holders,tgm-holders.empty-labels,tgm-indicators,
tgm-token-information,search-general,search-general.empty}.json` (+ `.evidence.md` siblings) —
  **provisional, spec-derived** (same two-step fixture strategy as M1's hand-built `dash-platform`
  fixture, PLAN §0.3): built strictly from the committed
  `docs/onchain-analytics/raw/nansen-openapi-2026-07-23.json`'s response schemas via targeted `jq`
  lookups (`SmartMoneyNetflowResponse`/`TGMHoldersResponse`/`TGMIndicatorsResponse`/
  `TGMTokenInformationResponse`/`GeneralSearchResponse`), never a live call. Every `.evidence.md`
  carries the literal line `provenance: spec-derived, NOT live` (task 005-4's own Phase 1
  verification greps for this — **note:** this required a small formatting fix to the two
  PRE-EXISTING `account.free`/`account.pro` evidence files from task 005-3 too, which had wrapped
  that phrase in markdown bold, `**spec-derived, NOT live**` — breaking the literal contiguous
  substring the grep needs; both evidence files' `provenance:` line lost its bold markup, no
  content/meaning change). `tgm-holders.empty-labels.json` deliberately omits `address_label` on
  its one holder row (R-32 "0 labels is valid, not an error") while `search-general.empty.json`
  returns `{tokens: [], entities: [], total_results: 0}` — together they golden-test
  `normalizeEntityLabels`'s empty-result path (TC-CONTRACT-03). Task 005-7 replaces all of these
  with genuinely recorded live responses; `nansen.contract.test.ts`'s own assertions are NOT to be
  edited when that happens (see this task's own `GOLDEN_TEST_SHA` note below).
- `test/nansen.contract.test.ts` (14 tests) — TC-CONTRACT-01 through 05 (golden normalization
  against the fixtures above, directly on `adapter.normalize(cap, raw)` — no network) + TC-UNIT-06
  through 12 (HTTP contract, against `adapter.fetch(cap, args)` with an injected, path-routing
  `fetchImpl`): headers carry `apiKey` never `authorization` (06), POST + `content-type:
application/json` + a JSON body with the exact expected fields (07), a redirect to a host outside
  the `api.nansen.ai` allowlist is rejected via the EXISTING `SsrfBlockedError` before it's ever
  followed (08), `429` → an error containing `"retry after 30"` with exactly one network call, no
  silent retry (09), `402` → a typed `NansenPaymentRequiredError` (10), a second sub-call throwing
  after the first succeeded rejects `fetch()` entirely with no partial result reaching `normalize()`
  (11), and — reusing the existing `CapabilityRegistry` integration pattern from `nansen.adapter
.test.ts` — no key means `isAvailable()` refuses BEFORE `registry.resolve()` ever reaches `fetch()`
  at all, proven with a `vi.fn()` spy asserted to have zero calls (12). Every test constructs its own
  `createThrottle()` instance (never the shared production singleton) — same test-isolation
  rationale as `budget-gate.ts`'s own precedent, avoiding real-timer waits against this provider's
  `capacity: 5, refillPerSec: 1` bucket across many test cases in one file.

### Task 005-5 (R-38/R-39): singleflight + budget-gate wiring + post-call reconciliation, composed inside `fetch()`

Test count rose from **295** (005-4's end state) to **310** — 15 new tests across two new files:
`test/nansen.singleflight.test.ts` (5, TC-UNIT-08..12) and `test/nansen.reconcile.test.ts` (10 —
TC-UNIT-01..04/07 plus one extra "unparseable, not just absent header" case, TC-UNIT-05/06, plus
two round-2 review tests, see below). Full detail on the two new modules
(`singleflight.ts`/`reconcile.ts`) and `index.ts`'s new composition lives in
`src/adapters/nansen/.AGENTS.md` (this task's own directory-level record — read it first, this
section only adds package-level context). In short:

- `fetch()`'s three layers, outside-in: `singleflight()` (always active, keyed by the existing
  `deriveArgsHash(capability, args)`) → the pre-call budget gate (`budget-gate.ts`'s
  `ensureBudget()`, task 005-3, genuinely called for the first time — ONLY when `deps.budgetStore`
  was supplied at `createNansenAdapter()` construction) → the unchanged task-005-4 sub-call routing
  (factored into a private `performSubCalls()`) → `reconcile.ts`'s post-call reconciliation, exactly
  once per logical call. A gate refusal throws BEFORE any reservation exists, so it's outside the
  `try/catch` that calls `accountState.markUnreconciled()` — that catch only wraps the
  sub-calls/reconcile step, where a reservation genuinely was already made.

> ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED by M-6 (adversarial cycle 1); restated vdd-multi cycle 4, G-3.** The two bullets
> below describe round 2's rule — "ungated requires an injected `fetchImpl`" — which the code no
> longer implements, and the real rule is **weaker** than they suggest. Consent is now an explicit
> boolean: `deps.budgetStore` omitted AND `deps.__ungatedForTestsOnly === true`, **the flag alone**
> (`fetchImpl` falls back to the real global `fetch`). Kept as the historical round-2 record.

- **`deps.budgetStore` omitted → `fetch()` falls back to the raw, ungated path, but ONLY when
  `deps.fetchImpl` was ALSO explicitly injected** (still singleflight-wrapped either way).
  `nansen.contract.test.ts` (task 005-4's own golden/HTTP-contract test, `GOLDEN_TEST_SHA`
  M-2-governed — see this file's own task-005-4 section above) constructs
  `createNansenAdapter({env, fetchImpl, now, throttle})` directly, with NO `budgetStore` but ALWAYS
  a fake `fetchImpl`, across a dozen HTTP-contract assertions; those tests are explicitly NOT in
  this task's "Правки" list and their SHA must stay byte-identical. Making the gate unconditional
  (e.g. defaulting a missing `budgetStore` to `createBudgetStore()`'s real disk-backed instance)
  would have (a) broken every one of those tests — the gate's cold-start resync would hit their
  fixture-routed `fetchImpl` at an unrouted `/api/v1/account` path and throw — and (b) silently
  written to the real, shared `DATA_DIR/cache.sqlite3` from ordinary unit tests.
  `packages/mcp-server/src/index.ts` (task 005-6) is this package's ONLY real production
  construction site and always supplies a real `budgetStore`, so THIS escape hatch is never
  reachable from `CapabilityRegistry` in production — confirmed re-verified (`git diff --stat --
packages/core/src/adapters/registry.ts` stayed empty throughout, same hard constraint as every M2
  task so far).
- **Round-2 code review — BLOCKING finding, fixed same day:** the FIRST version of the fallback
  above was conditioned on `budgetStore` ALONE — `deps.fetchImpl ?? fetch` inside it meant
  `budgetStore` omitted AND `fetchImpl` ALSO omitted (a real `NANSEN_API_KEY`, no injected
  transport at all) silently resolved to the REAL global `fetch`: a genuinely paid Nansen call with
  ZERO budget accounting, no throw, no stderr — the exact money-leak system-architecture.md §3.2
  OQ-2's "structurally non-bypassable" decision forbids ("non-bypassable if the caller remembers to
  pass `budgetStore`" is not the same property as "structurally non-bypassable"). **Fix:** the
  ungated branch now checks `deps.fetchImpl === undefined` FIRST and throws a clear refusal instead
  of proceeding — every legitimate ungated caller (by construction, since none may touch live
  network) ALWAYS injects a fake `fetchImpl` anyway, so this inverts the danger from "silently omit
  a param" to "deliberately opt in by injecting a fake transport". Proven by
  `test/nansen.reconcile.test.ts`'s new "OQ-2 structural non-bypassability" describe block (2
  tests): a real key + no `budgetStore` + no `fetchImpl` throws before ever reaching a
  `vi.spyOn(globalThis, 'fetch')`-instrumented real transport; the SAME construction WITH an
  injected `fetchImpl` (the legitimate escape hatch) still resolves normally.
- `nansen.contract.test.ts` — **untouched, verified byte-identical**
  (`shasum -a 256 packages/core/test/nansen.contract.test.ts` ==
  `c514c8ac129a568aa2e8330458d2a08928d5c75b4fd76c0999e2d1d6cd747cf8`, the same `GOLDEN_TEST_SHA`
  task 005-4 recorded, re-verified after the round-2 fix too) — confirming the conditional-gate
  design choice above kept it green without a single edit. None of
  `nansen.adapter.test.ts`/`nansen.budget-gate.test.ts`/`nansen.cost-of.test.ts`/
  `nansen.secrets.test.ts`/`nansen.types.test.ts` needed edits either.

**`registry.ts` — ZERO changes** (re-verified for this task, same hard constraint as
005-1..005-4): `git diff --stat -- packages/core/src/adapters/registry.ts` stayed empty throughout,
re-confirmed after the round-2 fix too. A gate refusal is an ordinary `throw` from
`adapter.fetch()`, indistinguishable to `resolve()` from any other adapter failure — its existing

**Mid-task correction, now fully resolved (important — read before trusting any earlier draft of
this section's own wording):** the FIRST pass through this task diagnosed the observed
`smart-money/netflow` empty result as "a deliberate vendor exclusion of base-pair/numeraire
tokens, not a defect." **That was wrong — TWO real code defects, both now fixed:**

1. `SmartMoneyNetflowFilters.include_stablecoins`/`.include_native_tokens` both default `false`

   lowercase-stored column (unlike its `/tgm/*` siblings, which accept the EIP-55 checksummed form
   fine) — `normalizeAddress()`'s canonical checksummed output silently matched nothing. Confirmed
   live via one isolated diagnostic call (identical request, only the address casing differed).
   Fixed: `postSmartMoneyNetflow` now lowercases `token_address` for this ONE vendor filter only.

Both fixes together produced a real, populated live response. `docs/issues/df-1-...md` status is
`fixed` (`resolved_by: TASK-005 / task 005-7`) — kept, not deleted, as a valuable "silent
empty-result" bug-class record, retitled away from the original false "base-pair tokens" framing.

## Q-2 + `/vdd-multi` cycle 4 (2026-07-25) — `NANSEN_DAILY_CREDIT_CAP` gets a default, and five defects

**Q-2 (commit `15b8dfa`) — the key now has THREE states**, not "optional / absent":

- **unset** → the gate DERIVES a ceiling: `deriveDailyCap(bucketStartBalance)` =
  `max(30, floor(balance × 0.25))`, resolved once per day-bucket and pinned into
  `NansenAccountSnapshot.dailyCapForBucket`. The guard is therefore ON out of the box.
- **positive integer** → an explicit operator ceiling. Applied DIRECTLY, never routed through the
  snapshot (`capInForce()`): a configured cap must not be able to disappear because a snapshot
  predates it — in a snapshot, `undefined` is indistinguishable from "off".
- **`off`** (the literal word) → no self-imposed ceiling; the vendor remainder alone bounds spend
  (the pre-Q-2 behaviour). `0` stays INVALID on purpose: it is one typo from silently disabling a
  money guard, and semantically ought to mean "spend nothing".

Either way `effectiveCeilingFor()` still takes `min(vendorCeiling, cap)`, so a cap can only ever
narrow, never widen. The ceiling in force is announced on stderr once per bucket.

**Cycle-4 defects found and fixed** (three parallel critics + an RTM completeness audit; every fix
carries a regression test that was verified to FAIL without it):

- **L-1 · `smart-money.flows` corrupted every Solana address.** The DF-1 fix applied
  `.toLowerCase()` to `filters.token_address` unconditionally, but it was live-verified on EVM only.
  Base58 is case-sensitive as an ENCODING (`chain/address.ts`'s `normalizeSolanaAddress()` returns
  its input unchanged for exactly that reason), so the fold corrupted the mint rather than re-casing
  it — recreating the DF-1 tarpit (empty `data: []` → post-payment throw → nothing cached → 10cr per
  retry) on the one advertised chain nobody probed. The fold is now EVM-only.
- **L-2 · the derived cap could lock the whole paid layer out for a UTC day.** It was derived from
  the live (post-spend) remainder but compared against `used`, a cumulative bucket counter — two
  reference frames. The per-bucket pin hid it _within_ a process; the pin is in memory only, so a
  mid-day MCP-server restart re-derived from a smaller balance and refused every paid call for the
  rest of the day. Now derived from the same `usageAtObserve + creditsRemainingAtObserve` anchor
  `effectiveCeilingFor()` already rebases onto, so every cold start, resync and concurrent session
  agrees on the number.
- **S-1 · `/account.credits_remaining` was accepted on `typeof` alone.** `1e999` is valid JSON and
  parses to `Infinity`; the value got PINNED, the ceiling became unbounded, and every reservation
  was approved for the rest of the bucket — unrepairable by a later healthy resync. Now requires a
  non-negative safe integer, matching the discipline `reconcile.ts` already applied to the far less
  consequential credits-used header.
- **L-5 · a `null` element in any vendor array destroyed an already-paid response.** Every row guard
  is shaped `typeof row.field !== 'string'`, which is itself a throw when `row` is `null`. Cycles
  1–3 closed this class for oversized strings, wrong types and fractional numbers; it stayed open
  one level up, at the element. `isRecord()` now guards each mapper, degrading to dropping the one
  bad row.
- **L-3 + L-4 · the success-path ledger write could nullify a paid call.** `await reconcile(...)`
  propagated, so `registry.ts` recorded a failure, nothing was cached, and the retry paid again
  (`SQLITE_BUSY` past the busy timeout is realistic — several stdio sessions share one
  `cache.sqlite3`). It is now best-effort: the reservation stays charged, `markUnreconciled()`
  fires — which is also where L-3 lived, since that call sat in the `!reconcileAttempted` branch and
  so never ran in exactly the case its own comment described — and the result is returned.

**Also corrected here: documentation that claimed more than the code delivered.** The
non-bypassability guarantee is "one explicit `__ungatedForTestsOnly` opt-in", NOT "two independent
conditions" (`fetchImpl` falls back to the real global `fetch`); the warn line is once per SNAPSHOT,
not per bucket; `budget-gate.ts` is wired into `fetch()` (since 005-5) and IS partially re-exported.

**Known and deliberately NOT fixed** (recorded, not claimed as done): no velocity limit — a daily
ceiling does not stop a burst (~50 credits/second at the current throttle); no negative caching, so
a legitimately-empty vendor response still costs a full price per retry; the 0-credit `entity.labels`
tier is structurally unrefusable by a credit-denominated gate; `/tgm/token-information` costs 1cr on
every `token.risk` call and its body is never read.

---

## L-1 + Q-4 (2026-07-25) — negative caching, and the sub-call we were paying for blind

**L-1 — `CapabilityRegistry.resolve()` now negative-caches deterministic failures.**

The old shape wrapped fetch+normalize in one `try`, and cached only after `normalize()` returned. So
every normalize throw discarded an already-fetched — on Nansen, already-PAID — response: nothing
cached, adapter recorded as failed, the caller's retry paying full price to be rejected identically.
10cr per `smart-money.flows` attempt, 100cr for the exhaustive `entity.labels` tier, on a loop with
no exit. The mechanism cost 35 real credits to discover (DF-1).

The split is the whole idea:

- **`fetch()` throws → never cached.** Transport, 429, 5xx, budget refusal — all transient. Caching
  that verdict turns a blip into a self-inflicted outage for the negative TTL.
- **`normalize()` throws → cached 60s.** Deterministic: the same body is rejected the same way, so
  a replay can only spend money to reach the same conclusion.

The caller's experience is unchanged — still `CapabilityUnavailableError`, never a fabricated empty
