**`src/adapters/blockscout/index.ts`** — the uptake, plus the **R-157 docstring obligation, honoured
in this same commit** because the old text itself demanded it ("this docstring must be rewritten in
the SAME commit, not after it"). `request()` forwards `deadlineAtMs` to `throttle()` (limiter first:
`entity.labels` costs 3 tokens against `{capacity: 5, refillPerSec: 2}`, so refusing a wait already
known to be longer than the time left is free, while discovering it afterwards costs the whole wait)
and to `safeFetch` — spread conditionally, so a call with no deadline builds byte-for-byte the
options object it built before. The new `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` docstring keeps the ≈410 s envelope
**as history** (it is the derivation `capability-manifest.ts` reuses rather than re-invents) and adds
the table the OD-3 error came from: `deadlineMs` bounds the ~140 s CANCELLABLE part (applied ceiling
60 s), `paidLegMs` the ~270 s UNCANCELLABLE paid leg (applied = measured), worst case ≈ 330 s. Any
reading of it as "the deadline bounds the whole call" is wrong in the expensive direction.

**`tdd-strict` (PLAN §0.9): 17 cases, 12 RED first by their declared `EXPECTED_FAIL_REASON`** — 7
resolved instead of rejecting, 3 read `undefined` where a moment was due, 1 fetched an adapter the
clock had ruled out, 1 (TC-INT-08b) hit vitest's own 5 000 ms timeout because nothing cancelled the
request. **4 were green from the first run and are labelled so in place** (TC-INT-04, TC-INT-05,
TC-INT-07, TC-INT-10), each carrying the mutation that kills it; TC-INT-11 is the 17th, added after
the mutation measurement above.

**Mutation-verified, each reverted byte-for-byte** (`diff` against a pristine copy): pre-check moved
BELOW the cache read → TC-INT-09 dies (the cached hit is returned instead of refused);
`DeadlineWouldExceedError` allowed to set `deadlineHit` → TC-INT-05 dies with `expected
CapabilityDeadlineExceededError … to be an instance of CapabilityUnavailableError`; the pre-check's
`hadFailure = true` deleted → TC-INT-02 dies by **RETURNING instead of throwing**, which is the exact
OD-4 violation; a past deadline folded into "absence" → TC-UNIT-04 dies; the terminal branch's second
disjunct deleted → nothing died before TC-INT-11 existed, everything green.

**Test mechanics.** `CapabilityRegistry` has NO injectable clock and does not get one (PLAN §0.3
fixes the seam count at three, none here), so the cases use a fake adapter that BURNS wall clock
synchronously against an INJECTED manifest whose `deadlineMs` is 20-50 ms — the fifth constructor
parameter 012-4 built. Spinning rather than sleeping keeps the adapter's duration the only variable;
every timing case keeps its two numbers ≥2x apart and the whole file runs in ~0.5 s. `blockscout`'s
uptake is proved BEHAVIOURALLY in two cases, not by a spy: `safeFetch` is a static import there and
`deadlineAtMs` is not part of `RequestInit`, so a `fetchImpl` spy cannot see it. TC-INT-08a drives the
bucket into DEFICIT first (two weighted pre-burns) — on a fresh bucket `throttle()` returns
synchronously and the case would pass even if the deadline never reached the limiter — and asserts
`error.at === 'provider "blockscout"'`, which is what separates the limiter's producer from the
transport's; TC-INT-08b asserts the class on `.cause` **and** an elapsed under 2 000 ms, since a
deadline that never reached the transport would surface as `SafeFetchTimeoutError` at
`REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000`.

**One pre-existing test was edited, and the plan said none would be.**
`test/registry.test.ts:85` asserted `toHaveBeenCalledWith('token.price', {address:'0xabc'})`, which
matches the FULL argument list — one more argument fails it. Changed to a third
`expect.any(Number)`, with the value itself asserted in `registry.deadline.test.ts` where the
manifest is injected. This is 012-8's own test blast radius, the analogue of what PLAN §H-D measured
for 012-1's `chains` deletion and §0.11 did not measure for this task.

## T-012 task 012-9 (2026-08-04) — the PAID boundary: after `checkAndReserve()`, the deadline is handed to nothing

Where 012-8 made the deadline travel, this task decides where it must STOP. `nansen` is the one
adapter that spends money, so D4 п.2 is a **correctness condition** here, not an optimisation:
cancelling work already paid for is "paid and did not receive", strictly worse than waiting for it.

**The boundary is `checkAndReserve()`, not the entry to `ensureBudget()`.** The free `/account`
resync runs INSIDE the gate, before the reservation, and it is roughly half of the measured
cancellable part of a call — cutting the deadline off at the gate's front door would deny it to
precisely the step the cancellable window exists for. So the deadline reaches the resync's limiter
wait AND its transport, and from the committed reservation onward it reaches nothing: not sub-call 1,
not sub-calls 2..N, not the limiter waits between them. `ensureBudget()` is called **once** per
logical `fetch()` and reserves the SUM of the 2–3 sub-call prices, so abandoning sub-call 2 is the
same failure one step later.

**Seam #1 (PLAN §0.3), built by owner decision OD-6: `safeFetchImpl?: typeof safeFetch`.** `safeFetch`
was a static import, and `fetchImpl` cannot substitute for it — `fetchImpl` receives a `RequestInit`,
which carries no `deadlineAtMs` — so the invariant was observable only on the limiter's half of the
path, i.e. the half that issues no request. The key is added to **three** deps types, all optional
(mirroring `throttle?`, never the mandatory `fetchImpl`, so no existing construction breaks):
`NansenEndpointDeps`, `NansenBudgetGateDeps`, and `NansenAdapterDeps` — the last because the endpoint
deps are built INSIDE `fetch()`, so without an adapter-level key the seam is unreachable from the
only supported construction path. One injected transport therefore observes BOTH legs of a call, and
the only difference between them is the argument this task is about.

**Seam #3: the singleflight follower** (`singleflight.ts`). `singleflight(key, fn, deadlineAtMs?)`
bounds the FOLLOWER's wait and nothing else. The leader's promise is never raced — its `fn()` is the
budget gate plus the paid sub-calls, and racing it is the forbidden outcome — while a follower whose
own deadline expires stops waiting and rejects with the **network** class `DeadlineExceededError`
(`CapabilityDeadlineExceededError` is not constructible in there: no `capability`, no `chain`, no
`tried`). The registry's existing C-1 catch translates it. The leader keeps running for every other
waiter, including one with a wider deadline. `deadlineAtMs` is deliberately NOT part of the key: two
calls for the same `(capability, args)` with different deadlines are one unfinished request.
`followUntilDeadline()` copies `safeFetch`'s own `raceWithTimeout` shape (an `AbortSignal.timeout`
plus a listener removed on settlement), so a leader rejection arriving after the follower gave up is
still consumed and can never surface as an unhandled rejection.

**WI-36 answered for `nansen`: it does NOT flatten the typed class, and that is now pinned.**
[WI-36](../../docs/backlog/wi-36-adapter-wrappers-flatten-typed-errors.md) measured two of twelve
adapters re-wrapping transport throws into `new Error(..., {cause})`, which makes `instanceof
DeadlineExceededError` false in the registry. Inheriting that on a paid route would leave the paid
deadline recognised only by the terminal branch's duplicate clock re-read. `nansen` is clean **by
construction, not by luck**: the vendor takes its key in an `apiKey` HEADER, so no URL carries a
secret and there is nothing for a wrapper to hide (`blockscout`'s reason does not apply), and
`index.ts`'s catch ends in a bare `throw error`. A repo-wide re-grep found **no third instance** —
`blockscout` has two wrap sites but is one adapter, and its second (`:402`, a body-read failure)
cannot produce our typed class — so the ledger needed no edit. The property is now held down by a
test rather than by this paragraph.

**No `deadlineAtMs <= now()` re-check was added before `checkAndReserve()`, and that is a decision.**
The registry already refuses to enter an adapter with a spent deadline, so such a check would be dead
on every production path; D4 п.2 asks for the deadline to be THREADED to the free work, not for a new
refusal to be invented at the till.

**`test/nansen-deadline-boundary.test.ts` (new, 9 cases) — a SEPARATE file on purpose.**
`nansen.contract.test.ts` is sha256-pinned in `docs/provenance.json` together with the nine
`test/fixtures/nansen/*` fixtures; appending to it would redden `verify:provenance` until a
re-baseline, i.e. this task would break the fixture-authenticity gate to make room for its own test.

**H3 is parameterised by registration PROPERTY, and the spying mechanism is named out loud.** The set
is `adapterRegistrations.filter(r => r.tier === 'paid')`. `dune` is excluded by the property
"`isAvailable()` is false even with every declared env var present" — asserted in its own case, so
the day it goes live it joins the contract set with no edit here — and never by its id. But the spies
are nansen-shaped, so a future paid adapter would fail on a MISSING SEAM rather than on a violated
invariant, and "the test is broken" is the wrong lesson: the file therefore carries an explicit
`PAID_ADAPTERS` map and fails with _"paid adapter X has no injectable transport seam — build it, per
OD-6"_. **TC-CONTRACT-02 is the mirror half** (before the reserve the deadline IS passed) without
which the suite cannot tell "the boundary holds" from "the deadline was never threaded at all".

**`tdd-strict` (PLAN §0.9).** Phase 1 (types declared, nothing plumbed) ran **4 of 9 RED** by their
declared `EXPECTED_FAIL_REASON`: TC-CONTRACT-01 on its own non-vacuity preamble (`expected 0 to be
greater than 0` — no transport observation exists yet), TC-CONTRACT-02 (`expected undefined to be
defined`), and TC-INT-03/TC-INT-04 as vitest 5 000 ms timeouts (the follower never rejects while the
leader is held). The other five are **green by construction and say so in place**, each with the
mutation that kills it.

**Mutation-verified, every one reverted and re-grepped**: deadline forwarded into the endpoint deps →
TC-CONTRACT-01 dies (`transport /api/v1/smart-money/netflow was handed a deadline AFTER the reserve`)
AND TC-INT-01/02 die with a real `DeadlineExceededError` on sub-call 2 plus a refund line on stderr —
one mutation showing both halves of the harm; deadline withheld from the resync's transport → and,
separately, from the resync's LIMITER only → TC-CONTRACT-02 dies each time, which is what proves both
halves are pinned independently; the follower's raced promise written back into the in-flight map (a
follower's expiry becoming the shared outcome) → TC-INT-03 dies as the wide follower loses the paid
result; `throw error` replaced by `throw new Error('nansen: transport failure', {cause: error})` →
the WI-36 case dies with `expected Error to be an instance of DeadlineExceededError`.

**Test mechanics, and why the clocks are mixed.** Deadlines are REAL `Date.now()` values because
`safeFetch`'s deadline arithmetic reads the real clock and has no seam; the LIMITER runs on a virtual
clock (`createThrottle(fakeClock())`) so no case burns a rate-limit wait. Expiry inside the paid leg
is driven by the fake transport itself spinning to `deadline + 1` (never a sleep), and each case
asserts afterwards that the call genuinely outlived its deadline. `account.free.json`'s recorded 74
credits give `effectiveCeiling = 30` (floor 30 binds), which admits every capability these cases
issue. **Two response-table entries deviate from the recording, deliberately and in a comment:**
`search/general`'s evidence records the credits header as ABSENT, which would route reconciliation
into its degrade path and hide the settled/refunded question behind a third outcome, so `0` (the
endpoint's real price) is sent instead; `search/entity-name` has no recorded fixture at all and its
body is read by nobody, so it answers with an empty envelope rather than a fixture this task is
forbidden to record.

**Scope note against PLAN §0.4.** The ownership table names
`src/adapters/nansen/{index,endpoints}.ts` for this task, but the boundary as the task file defines
it cannot be built there alone: the free `/account` resync lives in `budget-gate.ts` and the follower
discrimination is only possible inside `singleflight.ts` (from `index.ts` one cannot tell a leader
from a follower, and racing the shared promise would cancel the paid leg for everyone). Both files
are touched, and no other T-012 task claims either.

**Zero live credits (TC-REG-02).** `SELECT provider, day, credits_used FROM usage` on
`~/.onchain-intel/cache.sqlite3` returned **0 rows before and 0 rows after**, with all three DB files
byte-identical by MD5. Every store in the new file is `dbPath: ':memory:'`, every adapter gets an
explicit `env`, and no nansen fixture was recorded.

## T-012 task 012-10 (2026-08-04) — the mutation protocol, and AC-8 measured instead of computed

**This task adds no mechanism.** It checks the claims of the previous nine, and its equivalent of a
red phase is the mutation protocol itself: each of the four loaded deadline properties was switched
OFF in the source, the full suite run, the casualties recorded by name, and the edit reverted (each
revert verified by sha256 against a copy taken before the edit, not by eye).

**Four mutation runs, all on the full `pnpm test` (1414 green before, 1414 after every revert):**

| Property removed                                               | Where                                          | Dead                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| the walk stops at the deadline, with a typed outcome           | `registry.ts` pre-check + terminal branch      | **7** — `registry.deadline.test.ts` TC-INT-01/02/03/06/09/11 and `nansen-deadline-boundary.test.ts` TC-INT-04                         |
| the limiter wait is interrupted by the deadline (R-146)        | the `remainingMs` branches in `rate-limit.ts`  | **5** — `rate-limit.test.ts` TC-UNIT-08/09/10/11 and `registry.deadline.test.ts` TC-INT-08a                                           |
| the paid leg, once reserved, is handed no deadline (R-141)     | the endpoint deps built after `ensureBudget()` | **3** — `nansen-deadline-boundary.test.ts` TC-CONTRACT-01 (`limiter nansen was handed a deadline AFTER the reserve`) and TC-INT-01/02 |
| the caller cannot widen the deadline past the manifest (R-144) | the `min()` in `resolve()`                     | **1** — `registry.deadline.test.ts` TC-UNIT-01 (`expected 1785873143134 to be less than or equal to 1785869573139`)                   |

**An ineffective mutation is indistinguishable from a protected property, so each was proven to have
changed the executed path before its result was read.** Mutation 3 is the one that nearly slipped:
adding a key to a deps object that nothing consults leaves the suite green and looks exactly like a
pass. Its final form therefore imports the REAL `throttle`/`safeFetch` at runtime so the mutation
applies to the production path and not only to the injected test seams — confirmed by the failure's
own stack (`safeFetch → safeFetchImpl (index.ts:655) → callEndpoint → performSubCalls`).

**`test/entity-labels-deadline-arithmetic.test.ts` (new, 3 cases) — AC-8, on a virtual clock.** The
production chain (`blockscout` → `nansen` = free resync + paid leg) driven by real adapters, recorded
fixtures and one injected limiter whose clock IS `Date.now()`. The rule the file exists to obey:
every duration assertion is on **observed elapsed virtual time**, never on
`manifest.deadlineMs + manifest.paidLegMs` — a sum of two constants is green even on an engine with
no deadline at all.

- **TC-INT-01a — the ceiling cuts.** The cancellable script asks for 110 000 ms (blockscout's
  `MAX_WAIT_MS` + 4 × 5 000, then the resync's 4 × 15 000); the throw is observed at **65 000 ms**,
  with `/account` having run 1 of its 4 scripted hops, no reservation, `usage = 0`.
- **TC-INT-01b — the paid leg is untouchable.** The cancellable part fits (reserve observed at
  **35 000 ms**), the paid leg then runs its full **270 000 ms** (three sub-calls × (30 000 limiter +
  4 × 15 000)) and the throw is observed at **305 000 ms**. The load-bearing assertion is a
  DIFFERENCE between two observed moments (`throw − reserve`), which no arithmetic on manifest
  constants can produce; the credit stays settled at 5.
- **TC-INT-02 — kept as a recorded CONTROL NEGATIVE.** Same scenario with an instant nansen fixture:
  it stays green under mutation 3 AND mutation 1, so it proves nothing about AC-8 and says so in
  place. Both new cases die under mutation 1; 01b dies under mutation 3.

**What the virtual clock cannot do, stated because it bounds the numbers above.**
`vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ['Date'] })` moves `Date.now()` — which is the clock the registry,
`safeFetch` and `createThrottle` all read — but cannot fire `AbortSignal.timeout`, a native timer.
Production cuts a hop MID-flight with that signal; here the cut lands on the next `Date.now()`-based
check (`safe-fetch.ts:546` or the limiter's `remainingMs` branches). So 65 000 is the 60 000 ceiling
plus the tail of the hop in flight — an UPPER bound on production's cut, which is the safe direction:
an engine that ran longer still fails.

**Finding, recorded in `docs/architectures/open-questions.md`: PLAN §0.2's 140 s cancellable envelope
for `entity.labels` is not jointly realisable under the 60 s ceiling.** It sums two independent worst
cases whose limiter halves are 30 000 ms each; the limiter does not partially wait — with 10 000 ms
left it refuses a 30 000 ms backlog up front with `DeadlineWouldExceedError`, which is deliberately
NOT a deadline hit, so such a walk ends as `CapabilityUnavailableError`. The case therefore scripts
110 000 ms and observes 45 000 ms of it being cut. Nothing in the code is wrong; the envelope is an
upper bound over legs, not a duration one run can spend.

**Zero live credits.** `SELECT provider, day, credits_used FROM usage` on
`~/.onchain-intel/cache.sqlite3`: **0 rows before, 0 rows after**, the db file's sha256 unchanged
across the whole task including all four mutation runs.

## T-012 adversarial cycle 2 (2026-08-05) — nine findings, and what each one changed here

Three critics returned `issues-found` on the delivered T-012 tree. Two findings were live defects
with a probe behind them; the rest were claims the code did not hold. Recorded as a section rather
than as scattered comments because two of them (F-5, F-7) are **counting** corrections, and a count
that was wrong in five places is a class, not a typo.

- **F-1 — `net/safe-fetch.ts`, `raceWithTimeout`.** A caller signal already aborted on entry took an
  early-return branch that rejected BEFORE any handler was attached to the in-flight `fetchImpl`
  promise, which a real `fetch` rejects immediately with `AbortError`. On Node's default
  `--unhandled-rejections=throw` that ends a long-lived stdio server. Handlers are now attached
  first; `signal.aborted` is still read synchronously and still wins. Pinned by a case that installs
  a `process.on('unhandledRejection')` recorder and uses a **plain function**, not `vi.fn` — Vitest 4
  records each returned promise's settlement and thereby HANDLES it, so the mock version of the case
  passed against the unfixed code.
- **F-2 — `net/rate-limit.ts`.** The deadline refusal read `waitMs > remainingMs`, admitting the
  exact equality and every wait that leaves a sliver: the caller slept out its budget and
  `safeFetch` then produced the TERMINAL `DeadlineExceededError`, cancelling every adapter behind
  the saturated one — H-1 one floor down, from the branch written to prevent it. The test is now on
  what the wait LEAVES (`MIN_POST_WAIT_REMAINDER_MS = 5_000`, the shortest per-hop
  `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` any adapter configures). `TC-F2-INT` in `test/registry.deadline.test.ts`
  drives production's own numbers (31 s of ceiling, a 30 s backlog) and asserts that `nansen` is
  ASKED; against the old comparison it was not.
- **F-3 — `cache/sqlite-store.ts`.** Its `providers` upsert also set `notes = excluded.notes`
  (i.e. `NULL`) while `SqliteBudgetStore`'s did not, so constructing a cache store erased an
  operator's note and which value a `cache.sqlite3` held depended on which store opened it last —
  under a test whose own title said "byte-identical `providers` content" and whose reader selected
  two of the three columns. Conflict clause reduced to `kind`; the test helper now reads all three.
- **F-4 — `adapters/registry.ts` + `mcp-server`'s `budget-meta.ts`.** `_meta.budget` was derived
  from who ANSWERED, and on `entity.labels` the walk can enter and PAY `nansen` while returning
  `blockscout`'s answer (`unsatisfying ??=`). 012-3 had replaced a false positive with a false
  negative — the expensive direction, since an agent that believes a call was free repeats it.
  `CapabilityResolution` now carries **`attempted`** (adapter ids whose `fetch()` was entered, in
  walk order, omitted when empty), deliberately NOT filtered by tier: the registry states a
  traversal fact and the paidness classification stays where D8 put it.
- **F-5 — `capability-manifest.ts`.** Fourteen rows said the ceiling "cuts 75_000"/"cuts 35_000" and
  two said it was "the only in-process bound the pg leg's tail has at all". Measured over the built
  registry: **2 of 12 adapters** read `deadlineAtMs`, so **4 of 20 capabilities** are actually
  bounded. The file gained an ENFORCEMENT section with that measurement and every row now marks
  itself ENFORCED TODAY or DECLARED, not enforced. Behaviour unchanged — R-140e sanctions the
  staging; the defect was the claims. Gap tracked as **WI-37**.
  **The first pass of this fix reproduced the finding one level down, and the review caught it:**
  the markers were there, but in 11 of the 20 rows the phrase was wrapped across two comment lines
  or written as a one-off variant, so a reader greping one capability — or reading one row in a
  diff — found nothing, while the report said all twenty were marked. Both markers are now single
  canonical tokens (`**ENFORCED TODAY**`, `**DECLARED, not enforced today**`), the ~15 s banner
  names the eight rows it covers and disclaims the rest (`chain.supply` sits in the next block), and
  **TC-F5-GATE** in `test/capability-manifest.test.ts` makes the count executable: one marker per
  routed capability, each marker checked against a scan of `src/adapters/<id>/index.ts` for a code
  (not comment, not string) mention of `deadlineAtMs`, and the two ratios in the ENFORCEMENT prose
  compared with what the run measured. Proven by three mutations, each reverted: dropping
  `chain.supply`'s marker, flipping `token.price` to ENFORCED, and drifting the prose from 2 to 3
  adapters — each fails a different assertion of the gate. WI-37 cannot land without moving the
  markers and both ratios.
- **F-6 — `docs/backlog/wi-37-…md`.** The work item for threading the deadline into the remaining
  ten adapters, with the measurement, the reason it is not a regression, and the consequence for
  T-014/ADR-003 (a ceiling that is declared and not enforced cannot be an admission-control input).
- **F-7 — three narrowed claims.** (a) `src/index.ts` said mcp-server's tool layer reports the two
  deadline classes as different facts; `Deadline` appears **zero** times in that package's `src`.
  (b) `registry.ts`'s terminal branch said `DeadlineWouldExceedError` "cannot trip this OR" — true
  at the moment of the throw, but the disjunct is read later, so a one-adapter route refused with
  1 ms left does end as `CapabilityDeadlineExceededError`; the mirror note on the limiter is
  corrected too, and behaviour is unchanged. (c) "11 of the 12 adapters ignore it" was corrected to
  **10** in the four places that read as the current state.
- **F-8 — prototype keys and an unvalidated number.** `this.manifests[cap] === undefined` and
  `policyClasses[kind] === undefined` are false for `'constructor'`/`'toString'`/`'valueOf'`/
  `'hasOwnProperty'`: such a route built a registry whose deadline was silently `NaN` (and `>= NaN`
  is false at BOTH readers) and whose answer policy fail-opened to `Object`, disabling H-1 by way of
  a name. Both lookups now go through `Object.hasOwn`, and `manifest.deadlineMs` is validated with
  the same `Number.isSafeInteger` test `resolve()` already applied to the caller's own value —
  new `InvalidCapabilityManifestError`.
- **F-9 — the scanner lexer.** The "a quote opens a string only if it closes on the same line" rule
  existed in one of the three copies of `blankNonCode`; the other two consumed the rest of a file
  from a lone quote (a false NEGATIVE for every detector) and their DECLARED LIMITS tables did not
  say so. Rule ported into both, with a row in each table and an executable control that is red
  without it.

## WI-34 / WI-35 / WI-36 / WI-37 (2026-08-05) — the declared bounds became applied ones

Four backlog records, closed in one commit because they are four halves of two sentences: _the pg
read path had no bound of any kind_, and _the call ceiling reached two adapters out of twelve_. Each
was filed with "declared but not applied" as its class, and each is closed by making the code match
the declaration rather than by softening the declaration.

**`src/pg/read-client.ts` (WI-35)** — the query is bounded in time, by TWO numbers that stop
different things and therefore both exist:

- `statement_timeout = 5_000`, a `pg` config field sent as a startup parameter. **Server-side**: it
  cancels the statement and returns the connection to the pool. Derived, not chosen —
  `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` against the dev VM's `onchain.snapshots` (2 390 rows, 2026-08-05)
  measured **0.87 ms** for the four-metric query and **0.23 ms** for the one-metric one, so the bound
  is ~5 700× the worst measurement. The margin covers a cold buffer cache, a far larger table, and an
  unlucky plan — the four-metric query is ALREADY on a Seq Scan, so the shape that degrades with row
  count is the shipped one.
- `DEFAULT_QUERY_TOTAL_TIMEOUT_MS = 20_000`, an in-process race in this module. **Client-side**: it
  stops THIS process waiting, which is the only bound that survives a server that goes silent after
  the connection is established — the failure `statement_timeout` cannot reach, and the one that
  hangs a single-threaded stdio server whole rather than one capability. The number is a SUM
  constraint, not a preference: it must exceed `connectionTimeoutMillis` (10 000) + `statement_timeout`
  (5 000) or the two inner bounds become unreachable and three diagnosable failures collapse into one.
  `pg`'s own `query_timeout` is deliberately NOT also set — a second client-side timer with the same
  job and no discriminator, where whichever fired first would decide the message.

New `PgQueryTimeoutError`, kept distinct from the sanitized `pg-history: database unavailable`
rethrow for the reason `safe-fetch.ts` keeps its two classes apart. **The sanitize-and-rethrow catch
had WI-36's defect in it, found by the new test within the hour:** flattening our OWN typed refusal
into "database unavailable" hides the class from `instanceof` one layer up. The rule both places now
follow: sanitize what came from OUTSIDE, never what this module constructed.

**`src/adapters/pg-history/index.ts` (WI-34)** — `{capacity: 2, refillPerSec: 0.2}` has been in the
registration since it was written and no line of code read it. It is now applied. The pool's `max: 3`
bounds CONCURRENCY, which is a different quantity from RATE, so the pool was never the missing
limiter. Adding it made two tests in `pg-history.contract.test.ts` red on vitest's 5 000 ms timeout —
WI-26's failure, arriving on schedule — so every adapter in that file is constructed with
`isolatedThrottle()`.

**Gate (WI-34):** `throttle-seam.test.ts` gained a second population — every REGISTRATION, not just
every limiter-importer, since the first gate keys membership on the import and an adapter that
imports nothing was never in it. The exemption for `dune`/`dash-platform` is DERIVED (their `fetch()`
does nothing but throw `NotImplementedInM1Error`, and their `isAvailable()` is asserted
unconditionally false), where the record proposed a hand-written exception list: a derived exemption
lands a stub that grows a real `fetch()` in the population on the same commit. Proven by mutation —
removing `await throttle(…)` from `coingecko` fails it by name.

**`net/safe-fetch.ts` (WI-36)** — `PASS_THROUGH_TRANSPORT_ERRORS` + `isPassThroughTransportError`,
declared beside the classes rather than in each adapter's `catch`. `blockscout` and `blockchain-info`
rethrow those four classes as themselves and still wrap everything else. The wrapper's reason (M-7:
`?apikey=` in a URL that `safeFetch` interpolates into three of its own errors) survives untouched;
what changed is that a CLASS the registry decides on is no longer destroyed on the way out, so
`deadlineHit` is set on the `blockscout` path for the first time. Safety is a property of the LISTED
CLASSES — each redacts its own context at construction — and the gate in `safe-fetch.test.ts` checks
that per class AND requires every exported Error class to be on the list or knowingly off it, so the
next class has to make the decision instead of inheriting one.

**Ten adapters (WI-37)** — `coingecko`, `dexscreener`, `defillama`, `rpc-evm`, `rpc-solana`,
`platform-explorer`, `blockchain-info`, `pg-history` joined `blockscout` and `nansen`. Each forwards
`deadlineAtMs` unchanged to the limiter and to its transport, spread conditionally so a call without
one builds byte-for-byte the options object it built before. Three adapters needed more than the
template:

- **`rpc-evm` / `rpc-solana`** — the endpoint fallback loop `break`s on `DeadlineExceededError`. The
  loop exists because ONE endpoint can be down while another answers; our own time being up is true
  of every remaining one, so continuing would burn the list on entry checks and report the LAST
  endpoint's failure for a condition that had nothing to do with it.
- **`defillama`** — two mechanisms, because it has two shapes. `protocol.tvl` is a per-call request
  and gets the ordinary forwarding. `chain.tvl` and `dex.volume.history` are served from documents
  SHARED between concurrent callers, so `awaitSharedDocument` bounds THIS caller's wait and leaves the
  download running: handing the first caller's deadline to the `safeFetch` inside a shared body would
  let its expiry abort a download a second caller is also awaiting — H-1's shape, and what WI-12
  closed for in-flight cache entries. Both halves are tested, including that the second caller still
  receives the document the first abandoned.
- **`pg-history`** — the deadline reaches the limiter AND narrows the read client's in-process query
  bound, since a Postgres statement cannot be recalled once sent, only stopped being waited for.

**Exemption, and why it is not an id list.** `dune` and `dash-platform` spend no time at all
(`isAvailable()` unconditionally false, `fetch()` throws) — the same fact the manifest records as
E-DASH = 0. Giving them a `deadlineAtMs` parameter they ignore would buy the count by writing the
answer into the source, which is the decorative-seam pattern this package rejects everywhere else. So
`capability-manifest.test.ts` decides membership by whether an adapter imports a transport module at
all: the day a live gRPC transport lands for `dash-platform` (ARCHITECTURE.md §11), it enters the
population and the five rows it routes go red until it reads the deadline.

**Measured after, by the gates rather than by hand:** 10 of 12 adapters, **20 of 20** capabilities.
Every manifest row moved from `**DECLARED, not enforced today**` to `**ENFORCED TODAY**`; the marker
string stays in the file because TC-F5-GATE still expects it on any future row whose adapters do not
all read the deadline.

**Two envelopes changed, one of them upward, and that is the fix rather than a regression.** E-PG went
from "≥10 000 with an unbounded tail" to **50 000** (a 30 000 limiter wait that had never been counted
because the limiter was never called, plus the new 20 000 query bound), so both `*.history` rows now
read `measured envelope: 140_000 … applied: 30_000 … cuts 110_000`. A larger measured number here
means the thing being measured acquired a bound.

**New file `test/deadline-uptake.test.ts`** — the TC-INT-08a and TC-INT-08b forms, once per adapter
(a spent deadline refused BY THE LIMITER with no request at all, asserted through
`at === 'provider "<id>"'`; and a live deadline beating the hop timeout by 40×, asserted through
class AND elapsed), plus the gate WI-37 asked for: every registration constructed with a recording
throttle, required to forward the caller's deadline UNCHANGED. Its population check names `nansen` as
covered elsewhere rather than letting it be silently absent. Both halves proven by mutation —
dropping the limiter argument in `platform-explorer` and the transport argument in `dexscreener` each
fail a different case.

## T-013 task 013-1 (2026-08-06) — `mergeable?: boolean` lands on `set | series` alone; T-012's obligation discharged

The field 012-4's docstring described as future (`## T-012 task 012-4`, `- TC-UNIT-07 guards the
DECLARATION, not the merge rule` above) now exists: `mergeable?: boolean` on the `set | series`
branch of `CapabilityManifest`, `mergeKey?: never` on `point` explicitly rejected (task file F-5) in
favor of not pre-picking a name T-013 had not chosen. **That older bullet's closing sentence — "a
test claiming 'merging cannot be declared on `point`' would be FALSE here and is not written" — is
now stale for the field that exists; TC-UNIT-09 below is exactly that test, and it is written.** Left
as dated journal prose rather than edited, per this project's convention that entries describe the
state AT THE TIME; this entry is the correction a reader lands on next.

`privacy.shielded_pool.history` and `platform.metrics.history` get `mergeable: true`; the other 18
rows, including `entity.labels` (the read most plausible by mistake — `set`-shaped, the most
expensive call in the system), do not. Three new tests beside TC-UNIT-07: **TC-UNIT-09**
(`@ts-expect-error` — `mergeable` on `point` does not compile), **TC-UNIT-10** (positive control —
`mergeable` on `series` compiles, no directive), **TC-UNIT-11** (only the two `.history` rows carry
it in the real table). **AC-22's mutation protocol, run and recorded here** (there is no
`docs/reports/` in this project and no standalone 013-1 report file — this entry is the durable
record; a chat transcript to the orchestrator is not one). Step 1: moved `mergeable?: boolean` onto
`CapabilityManifestBase`. Step 2, `pnpm --filter @onchain-intel/core typecheck`:

```
$ tsc --noEmit
test/capability-manifest.test.ts(403,7): error TS2578: Unused '@ts-expect-error' directive.
```

Failed exactly as required — TC-UNIT-09's directive, now unused because `mergeable` is legal on
`point` too. Step 3, reverted, same command:

```
$ tsc --noEmit
```

Clean.

**`unionBranches()` (`test/capability-manifest.test.ts`, TC-UNIT-07's own scanner) had a CRITICAL
defect this task's own diff exposed and an adversarial round caught: its terminator search was
`source.indexOf(';', start)` — the first `;` in the text, not the first one at bracket depth 0.**
Once `mergeable` gave the `set | series` branch its own multi-line object type
(`{ shape: 'set' | 'series'; mergeable?: boolean; }`), that branch's OWN property-separating `;` was
mistaken for the union's terminator: everything after it — the field, the branch's closing `})` — was
silently invisible to the guard, which still reported branch count 2 **by luck** (the truncated tail
was non-empty) and TC-UNIT-07 passed while unable to detect a real third branch appended after the
truncation point (measured by injecting one — `unionBranches` returns fewer characters than the
source contains). Fixed: comments are stripped first (`stripComments`), then the terminator is the
first `;` at depth 0, scanned character-by-character rather than located by `indexOf`. Regression
fixture added to `'the guard DETECTS each reached form'` — a third branch hidden behind a multi-line
second branch carrying its own `;` — plus a sign-of-work assertion on the real declaration that the
extracted second branch reaches its actual terminator (`mergeable` and a trailing `})`), not the
truncation point. **Lesson for the next field added to either branch:** a scanner whose terminator
search does not track bracket depth is safe only as long as no branch grows a semicolon of its own —
which is precisely what the FIRST field added to this union did.

## T-013 task 013-2 (2026-08-06) — `CapabilityRoute.merge`, the second constructor validation step, and `assertMergeParticipantsAreFree`

Two independent axes, per PLAN §0.7/OQ-T013-2/OQ-T013-3 (both closed by Architecture 2026-08-05):
`capabilityManifests[cap].mergeable` (013-1) is a fact about the CAPABILITY's key identity;
`CapabilityRoute.merge?: boolean` (this task, `adapters/types.ts`) TURNS merging on per ROUTE.
`CapabilityRegistry`'s constructor grows a **step 3**, after step 1 and independent of step 2: if
`route.merge === true`, the manifest row step 1 already found must carry `mergeable: true`, else
`MergeEligibilityNotDeclaredError` (`registry.ts`, by the `UnregisteredPolicyClassError` pattern) —
names the capability and the missing eligibility (UC-20, R-183). A route broken by BOTH
`policy.kind` (step 2) and `merge` (step 3) reports step 2's finding — a DETERMINISTIC tie-break
(three sequential loops over `this.routes`, step 2's throws before step 3's ever runs), not a claim
that one defect matters more.

**`assertMergeParticipantsAreFree(routes, registrations)`** lives beside
`assertValidAdapterRegistrations` in `adapters/types.ts` — **never in the constructor** (PLAN §0.7):
`013-4`'s own R-166 test builds a merge route pairing a free and a paid adapter to observe walk
ORDER, and a constructor-embedded check would make that fixture unconstructible. Rule: for every
capability with ≥1 route carrying `merge: true`, every id in the UNION of `adapterIds` across ALL of
that capability's routes must be `tier: 'free'` — the union, not one route's own list, because
`resolve()`'s `plan` already unions every matching route's adapters (a paid adapter reachable only
through a sibling non-merge route would otherwise enter the merged walk unverified). Reads `tier`,
never `.trust`. Exported from `core/src/index.ts` (same idiom as `assertValidAdapterRegistrations`);
called from `mcp-server/src/index.ts` immediately after `assertValidAdapterRegistrations` and before
any store/registry is constructed.

**No new rank structure.** The merge conflict rank IS `adapterIds`' own compiled order — the same
one `resolve()`'s pre-existing `plan` already builds (unmodified by this task; the walk itself is
013-4). Documented as PROVISIONAL on `CapabilityRoute.merge`'s own docstring, replacement addressed
to T-016 (OQ-T013-3 owner decision, 2026-08-05).

**Stub-First, captured verbatim (no `docs/reports/` in this project — see the 013-1 entry above for
why this entry is the durable record).** Phase 1: the `merge` field, `MergeEligibilityNotDeclaredError`,
step 3's loop shape (never throws), and `assertMergeParticipantsAreFree`'s signature (never throws) —
all tests written against that tree. Red run, `packages/core/test/merge-activation.test.ts`:

```
 ❯ test/merge-activation.test.ts (9 tests | 2 failed) 30ms
     × throws MergeEligibilityNotDeclaredError naming the capability and the missing eligibility
     × names the capability and the paid adapter; fails when the check reads one route's
       adapterIds instead of the union
 Test Files  1 failed (1)
      Tests  2 failed | 7 passed (9)
```

`packages/mcp-server/test/merge-participants-startup.integration.test.ts` (TC-INT-05), same tree:

```
AssertionError: expected undefined to be an instance of Error
 Test Files  1 failed (1)
      Tests  1 failed (1)
```

Phase 2 (the eligibility check's body, the union-walk body): all 9 + 1 green.

**A pre-existing gate caught a real false positive along the way — and the first fix for it was
itself superseded by review round 1's M-2 (see below).** The first Phase-2 draft of
`assertMergeParticipantsAreFree` built its id→tier lookup with
`if (!tierById.has(registration.id)) tierById.set(registration.id, registration.tier);` — legitimate
(reads `registration.tier` directly, per ADR-002 D8), but `tier-single-source.test.ts`'s OWN
`TC-GATE-01` (task 012-3, R-151(e) — an unrelated gate that happens to share a TC id with this task's
grep gate below) flagged it: `tierById` splits into `tier`/`by`/`id` under the scanner's camelCase
tokenizer, so a `.has(id)` membership test sharing a statement with `registration.tier` reads as a
second paidness-by-id classification. The FIRST fix simply dropped the `.has()` guard entirely
(unconditional `.set()`, last-registration-wins) — a deletion of the id-test, not the "split across
statements" escape hatch this paragraph originally (and wrongly) credited it with; see M-2 below for
why last-wins was ALSO wrong on its own terms, and for the fix that genuinely does split the id-test
from the paidness vocabulary across two statements. Verbatim, the ORIGINAL collision this avoided:

```
AssertionError: A second classification of paidness appeared. `AdapterRegistration.tier`
(ADR-002 D8) is the only one: read it, do not rebuild it from provider ids.:
- []
+ [
+   "packages/core/src/adapters/types.ts: if (!tierById.has(registration.id))
      tierById.set(registration.id, registration.tier)",
+ ]
```

`tier-single-source.test.ts`'s `TC-GATE-02` (`.trust` has no reader) is green with **zero edits to
its file** — the new code reads `tier`, never `.trust`.

**TC-GATE-01 (this task's own) — grep for `onchain.metrics.source_priority`, positive control run
and recorded.** A temporary one-line comment mentioning the string was added to
`adapters/policy.ts`, reverted immediately after capturing the failure (`git diff` on that file is
empty afterward). Red:

```
AssertionError: expected [ Array(1) ] to deeply equal []
- []
+ [
+   "packages/core/src/adapters/policy.ts",
+ ]
 Test Files  1 failed (1)
      Tests  1 failed | 8 skipped (9)
```

Green after revert: `Tests  1 passed | 8 skipped (9)`.

**TC-INT-05's mutation proof.** The `assertMergeParticipantsAreFree(routes, adapterRegistrations);`
call line was removed from `mcp-server/src/index.ts`, the integration test re-run alone
(`expected undefined to be an instance of Error`, `Tests  1 failed (1)`), then the line restored —
`git diff --stat` on that file afterward shows exactly the intended `+11` insertion, nothing else.
Without this test, TC-UNIT-01/02 above stay green while the check is declared but unenforced at the
one place that matters — the same WI-34…WI-37 distinction task 012-2's own TC-INT-01 exists for.

Final counts: core 1209 → 1218 (+9), mcp-server 332 → 333 (+1); all six PLAN §0.2 gates green;
`tools-list.snapshot.json` diff empty.

### Review round 1 (2026-08-06) — REJECTED, 0 CRITICAL/0 HIGH/2 MED/7 LOW; both MEDs and four LOWs fixed

**M-2 — the "last-registration-wins" fix above was itself FAIL-OPEN.** On a duplicate id
(`[{id:'x',tier:'paid'},{id:'x',tier:'free'}]`) first-wins throws, last-wins passes a paid
participant through the gate that exists to refuse one — neither direction is fail-closed, and the
comment justifying last-wins by delegating to `adapter-registrations.test.ts` ("that file's own
concern") was checked and found false: that file has no id-uniqueness test at all (its own TC-UNIT-05
builds its expectation via `Object.fromEntries(...)`, which itself collapses a duplicate id last-wins
— the same defect, restated as a citation). Fixed to STICKY-PAID: `const known =
tierById.get(registration.id); tierById.set(registration.id, known === 'paid' ? known :
registration.tier);` — once any registration for an id says `paid`, it stays `paid` regardless of
array order. Two NEW regression cases in `merge-activation.test.ts` assert both orderings throw.
Confirmed against `tier-single-source.test.ts`: `.get`/`.set` are outside its MEMBERSHIP
alternation and `known`'s last segment is not an `ID_WORDS` token — **not**, as this paragraph
originally (and wrongly) also claimed, because the id-bearing read sits in its own statement away
from the paidness-bearing one (review round 2, LOW-3): the `.set()` call itself names both
`registration.id` and `registration.tier` together, text-adjacent, in the very same statement. The
gate passes for the two real reasons stated, not for that invented third one — dropped here rather
than left to mislead the next reader who checks it against the code.

**Superseded by round 2's LOW-1 below**: `known === 'paid'` was itself replaced with `known !==
'free'` (sticky-NON-free, not sticky-paid) once a third, cast-borne tier value was shown to defeat
this exact line. Read this paragraph as the state at the time; the section below is current.

```
$ pnpm --filter @onchain-intel/core exec vitest run test/tier-single-source.test.ts
 Test Files  1 passed (1)
      Tests  14 passed (14)
```

**M-1 — R-163's "empty `adapterIds` already rejected upstream" premise is false about this tree.**
Measured (`rg -n "adapterIds\.length|adapterIds: \[\]|EmptyRoute|adapterIds\.every|adapterIds\.some"
packages/` — one unrelated hit): no such check exists anywhere. A route
`{capability:'platform.metrics.history', adapterIds: [], merge: true}` passes step 3 (which never
reads `adapterIds` at all) and passes `assertMergeParticipantsAreFree` vacuously (empty participant
set, zero iterations). No runtime harm — the harm was four documents asserting a check that isn't
there, the WI-38/WI-42 class. Fixed by NOT implementing the unneeded rejection and instead: (1) two
new tests in `merge-activation.test.ts` pin the actual permissive behaviour of both mechanisms; (2)
all FOUR instances of the false premise corrected (a broader sweep than the two the critic cited,
per the m3 "check the denominator" lesson) — `docs/architectures/system-architecture.md:839`,
`docs/PLAN.md` §0.5 item 2 and its R-163 RTM line, and `docs/TASK.md`'s R-163(b) — to state the TRUE
constructive argument: every participant is an element of `adapterIds`, so it has an index, so it
has a rank; zero participants is the vacuous case of that claim, not a case a rejection guards.

**Four LOWs, also fixed:** L-1 (`docs/tasks/task-013-2-route-activation-and-rank.md:53` said the
constructor builds `plan`; corrected to `resolve()`, `registry.ts:638-650` (off-by-one from
`:638-649` caught and fixed in review round 2), matching the source docstrings already fixed in the
first pass — same fact, one more of the 3-4 places it lived). L-2
(`registry.ts`'s step 3 claimed a `point` row "can never read `true` here … by type" — true for a
compile-time literal, false for a constructor-injected cast carrying both `shape:'point'` and a
stray `mergeable:true`; added `manifest.shape !== 'point' &&` to the runtime check itself rather than
just narrowing the comment, since `manifests` is explicitly constructor-injected, the same F-8 threat
model as the `Object.hasOwn` lookup two lines above it). L-3 (TC-INT-04's one-route, both-axes-broken
fixture could not distinguish three sequential loops from one merged per-route loop checking
policy-then-merge — both shapes report the SAME route's policy error first; rewritten to two routes,
each broken on a single, DIFFERENT axis, which only the real three-loop implementation resolves to
`UnregisteredPolicyClassError`). L-5 (the `source_priority` grep gate's positive control was a manual
mutation with no permanent proof of detection capability; added a second, permanent `it()` pointing
the same `walk()` at `packages/core/test` — which holds the literal in this very file — asserting
≥1 hit, per `tier-single-source.test.ts`'s own stated rule that "a detector without one can be added
dead"). Skipped (carryable, per the critic): L-4, L-6, L-7.

Final counts after round 1: core 1218 → 1222 (+4: one M-2 regression, two M-1 pinning cases, one
L-5 positive control; L-3 rewrote an existing case in place), mcp-server unchanged at 333. All six
PLAN §0.2 gates green again, `tier-single-source.test.ts` 14/14, `tools-list.snapshot.json` diff
empty.

### Review round 2/3 (2026-08-06) — round 2 REJECTED (1 blocking MED, 8 LOW); round 3 fixes closed it

**MED-1 (blocking) — L-2's `manifest.shape !== 'point'` guard had no test exercising it, so two
mutants of step 3's four-clause `eligible` expression both survived a full-suite run.** Mutant A
(drop `'mergeable' in manifest && manifest.mergeable === true`, keep only the shape check) and
Mutant B (drop `manifest.shape !== 'point'`, keep only the declaration check) each left `Tests 1222
passed`. Mutant A is the serious one: R-183/UC-20's realistic instance is a `series` capability
whose manifest simply lacks `mergeable` (optional since 013-1, the DEFAULT state of a new series
row), and no fixture in the tree exercised it — every `merge: true` case paired `point`+no-eligibility
or `series`+eligible. Two new cases added to TC-INT-01's `describe` in `merge-activation.test.ts`:
(a) `series` with no `mergeable` field at all, (b) a cast-borne `{shape:'point', mergeable:true} as
unknown as CapabilityManifest`. Both mutants re-applied and re-run — each now kills exactly its own
new case, nothing else:

```
Mutant A: Tests  1 failed | 14 passed (15) — kills case (a) only
Mutant B: Tests  1 failed | 14 passed (15) — kills case (b) only
```

Reverted; full file green at 15/15.

**LOW-1 — sticky-PAID (round 1's M-2 fix) was still order-dependent under a THIRD tier value.**
`AdapterTier` is `'free' | 'paid'` at the type level but this function reads a runtime-assembled
array; a cast-borne `tier: 'trial'` duplicate could be silently overwritten back to `'free'`
depending on array order, since the old line only special-cased the literal `'paid'`. Changed to
sticky-NON-FREE: `known !== undefined && known !== 'free' ? known : registration.tier` — it does
not ask what the sticky value IS, only whether it has been confirmed `'free'`. New regression test
asserts both orderings of a `'trial'`/`'free'` duplicate throw. Re-ran `tier-single-source.test.ts`:
14/14, no new collision.

**LOW-2/3 — cheap, both fixed.** `.toThrow(/dup/)` matched the boilerplate word "de**dup**" in the
error message regardless of cause; changed to `.toThrow(/participant 'dup'/)`. The comment claiming
the id-bearing read "never shares a statement with `registration.tier`" was false (the `.set()` call
itself names both, text-adjacent) — dropped, keeping only the two reasons that are actually true
(`.get`/`.set` outside the scanner's MEMBERSHIP alternation; `known` not an `ID_WORDS` operand). The
same false claim, repeated in this file's own round-1 entry, corrected there too rather than left as
"dated journal prose" — it was never true, at any date.

**LOW-4 — the diff's own blast radius on `docs/architectures/system-architecture.md`'s line
citations turned out to be much larger than the 7 the critic sampled.** A full sweep of every bare
`` `:NNN` ``/`` `:NNN-NNN` `` anchor in the file found a dense cluster of 20 numeric citations
(lines 877, 906, 914, 924, 932–936, 939–942, 952) all describing `registry.ts` content at or after
old line 375 — every one of them measured against the pre-diff baseline (`git show HEAD:...`) and
found ACCURATE before this task's `+87`-line diff, hence genuinely invalidated by it. All 20
corrected together (uniform `+87` — confirmed there is exactly one shift constant for the whole
range, since every insertion in this diff sits above old line 375). **Six sibling citations found in
the same document, checked against the SAME pre-diff baseline, were already wrong before this task
touched anything** — `:191-195`/`:238-254`/`registry.ts:384`/`:453`/`registry.ts, ~372-391` (×2) cite
content (a cache-entry interface, a `CapabilityResolution` docstring, a `getCoverage()` call, a
`resolve()` parameter) unrelated to what the surrounding prose describes, in the baseline itself —
pre-existing drift from earlier tasks, out of this diff's blast radius, left alone. Two more
`638-649` off-by-one echoes of the same L-1 fact (this file's own round-1 entry, and the task file
itself) corrected to `638-650` alongside.

**The "20 of 26" this entry first reported was itself a wrong denominator — the third in a row on
this task, and the reason the count is now stated with its SCOPE rather than as a total.** Roast
round 3 found the sweep had been bounded to `registry.ts` anchors inside one document, so it missed
`system-architecture.md:346` (`types.ts:214-262` → `333-416`), broken by this same diff and left
uncorrected, and every anchor OUTSIDE that document. Corrected by the orchestrator after the roast
loop was exhausted, on the owner's decision: the four `docs/PLAN.md` coordinates that **013-3 and
013-5 are planned against** (`registry.ts:944` → `:1031` twice — one of them the target of 013-5's
own mutation protocol, which would otherwise have deleted a comment and "passed"; `types.ts:214` →
`:333`; `registry.ts:210` → `:253`) plus `system-architecture.md:346`.

**What is still stale is recorded, not silently carried:** `reliability.md:143/144/147/153/155` and

drifting", and now drifts) are filed as a work-item rather than fixed here — they are outside this

`system-architecture.md`); 6 pre-existing wrong left alone; 6 known-stale in other files filed.**
The lesson is not the arithmetic — it is that a sweep reports the scope it searched, because a
denominator without its scope is indistinguishable from a complete one.

## T-013 task 013-3 (2026-08-06) — three optional `CapabilityResolution` fields, strictly additive

Phase 1 of the response-shape task (R-174/R-175): `CapabilityResolution` (`registry.ts:253`) gains
`sources?: string[]`, `missingSources?: { adapterId: string; reason: string }[]` and
`perSourceCache?: { adapterId: string; cache: 'hit' | 'miss'; ageMs?: number }[]`, declared right
after `deadlineOverrunMs` and documented at the same density (the merge walk that actually
populates `sources`/`perSourceCache` is 013-4; `missingSources` is 013-5 — this task's own `resolve()`
diff is `git diff --stat -- packages/core/src/adapters/registry.ts` = additions only, confirmed
below). `source` stays required and a single string, `cache` stays the two-literal `'hit' | 'miss'`
(R-175b forbids widening it) — neither touched. No new named type extracted (the task file's own
field declarations are inline object-literal types; `missingSources`'s per-item shape happens to
match the pre-existing `CapabilityAttempt` — deliberately NOT reused: `tried[]`'s error-diagnostic
concept and a success-shaped "who is missing" list are different things that happen to look alike
today, and coupling them would make a future divergence a breaking change for the wrong reason).

**`perSourceCache` covers every ANSWERED participant, not only `sources` (contributors) — a
deliberate deviation from FOUR architecture sentences (found three at first pass; a fourth,
`system-architecture.md:947-949`, surfaced by roast round 1's B-4 — the procedural "Merge
mechanism" paragraph couples `perSourceCache` to the same `satisfied` condition as `sources`),
argued in full and dated in `docs/architectures/open-questions.md` ("T-013 task 013-3"), with all
four sentences marked in place (`system-architecture.md:980-981`/`:990`/`:947-949`,
`interfaces.md:474-481` — the first two share one mark, `:947-949` gets its own, separate mark
elsewhere in the same file).** Short form: a cache status is a fact about the ANSWER, not the
CONTRIBUTION (R-174(c)), and the
contributors-only reading would drop a zero-point, cache-answered participant from `_meta.cache`
entirely — silently erasing that it was asked at all.

**TC-UNIT-04 (`test/registry.test.ts`) — the type-test, POSITIVE control, no `@ts-expect-error`.
It has a NEGATIVE sibling in the same file since roast round 1 (B-3): a `@ts-expect-error` case
proving `perSourceCache[].cache` rejects a third literal, because TC-UNIT-04 alone cannot fail on a
widening — it constructs no third-literal value.**
(mirrors `capability-manifest.test.ts`'s TC-UNIT-10): a `CapabilityResolution` literal carrying
none of the three new fields still assigns. Mutation proof, captured verbatim — `sources?` narrowed
to `sources` (required) in `registry.ts`, `pnpm --filter @onchain-intel/core typecheck`:

```
src/adapters/registry.ts(919,15): error TS2741: Property 'sources' is missing in type '{ result: unknown; source: string; cache: "hit"; ageMs: number; }' but required in type 'CapabilityResolution'.
src/adapters/registry.ts(993,15): error TS2741: Property 'sources' is missing in type '{ result: unknown; source: string; cache: "miss"; }' but required in type 'CapabilityResolution'.
test/registry.test.ts(574,11): error TS2741: Property 'sources' is missing in type '{ result: { priceUsd: number; }; source: string; cache: "miss"; }' but required in type 'CapabilityResolution'.
```

**Transcript RE-CAPTURED in roast round 2, not hand-edited.** The first capture recorded `(912,15)`
and `(986,15)`; the fix pass then inserted a 7-line docstring paragraph at `registry.ts:330-336`,
shifting both by `+7`. A transcript labelled "captured verbatim" that no longer reproduces is worse
than no transcript, so the mutation was re-run rather than the numbers retyped — same defect class
as the citation decay this very task was fixing (WI-43), recurring inside the fix for it.

Notable beyond the test itself: the SAME mutant also breaks `resolve()`'s own two internal
`CapabilityResolution` object literals (the cache-hit path, `:919`, `const hit: CapabilityResolution
= {`, and the fresh-answer path, `:993`, `const answer: CapabilityResolution = {`) — this task's

No `docs/reports/` in this project (see the 013-1 entry above for why this journal entry is the

## T-013 task 013-4 (2026-08-06) — the merge walk: dedup on `(metric, asset, ts)`, rank conflict resolution, per-adapter cache, per-participant policy

**Branch, don't fork.** `const mergeMode = matching.some((route) => route.merge === true)` sits right
after `plan`, and the merge walk is a sibling block ahead of the single-winner loop, which is
**untouched**. `.some(...)` rather than "first matching route" or "every matching route": a
capability served by several routes disagreeing on `merge` is out of scope (PLAN §0.5 item 5) and no
route-agreement check exists anywhere in this tree.

**What the merge walk reuses verbatim** — deadline pre-check, adapter lookup, chain-scoped skip,
`isAvailable()`, cache read including its negative-entry branch, and the `fetch`/`normalize`/
`cache.set` triad. The single difference, in two places: the early
`return withDiagnostics(...)` becomes "record the answer, accumulate if it satisfies, keep walking".
`unsatisfying` is deliberately never touched on this path — returning one participant's raw answer
would un-merge exactly the answer that was requested merged (TC-INT-12).

**`hadFailure`/`deadlineHit` are deliberately NOT written in this block.** 013-4 always returns a
merged success, so nothing here reads them and writing them would be a dead store — `lint`'s
`no-useless-assignment` catches exactly that, correctly. 013-5 reintroduces the assignments together
with the outcome contract that consumes them.

### Mutation protocol — eight mutants, run and captured. **Three survived and are now closed.**

Run on `test/registry.merge.test.ts` unless noted; every mutant reverted from a byte backup, tree
re-verified clean after each.

| #   | Mutation                                               | Result                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| M1  | drop `if (merged.has(key)) continue;` (Map overwrites) | `3 failed \| 13 passed (16)`                           |
| M2  | key drops `metric`                                     | `1 failed \| 15 passed (16)`                           |
| M3  | key drops `ts`                                         | **`16 passed (16)` — SURVIVED**                        |
| M4  | walk `plan` in reverse rank order                      | `9 failed \| 24 passed (33)` (with `merge-activation`) |
| M5  | `perSourceCache` filtered to contributors              | `1 failed \| 32 passed (33)`                           |
| M6  | key drops `asset`                                      | **`16 passed (16)` — SURVIVED**                        |
| M7  | key drops both `asset` and `ts` (bare `metric`)        | **`16 passed (16)` — SURVIVED**                        |
| M8  | `cache.set()` called on a cache HIT                    | `2 failed \| 14 passed (16)`                           |

**M2 was not a hypothetical — it was live in the tree.** The agent building this task was
interrupted mid-protocol with the mutation still applied (`// MUTATION 2 (temporary…)` left in
`mergeInto`), and `pnpm test` came back `1 failed | 1243 passed`, failing exactly TC-INT-02. That is
the strongest evidence a test can produce, and it is also why RF-7 (roast and mutation protocol
sharing one working tree, with no rule sequencing them) was worth filing.

**Why M3/M6/M7 survived — one root cause, and the dangerous one.** AC-1 names the key as _exactly_
the triple `(metric, asset, ts)`. TC-INT-02 pinned `metric`; TC-INT-03 pinned that no EXTRA field is
in the key. Nothing pinned the other two thirds, because `point()` hard-codes
`asset: DASH_PLATFORM_ASSET` and every fixture used a single `ts`. So the key could be reduced to
bare `metric` with all 16 tests green.

The `ts` case is not exotic — it is the failure this whole capability exists to avoid. A `series` is
points over TIME; a key without `ts` collapses every hour of one metric into whichever hour the
highest-ranked participant returned first. The merge would silently destroy the series it exists to
assemble, on the ordinary two-participant call, with the suite green.

Closed by **TC-INT-03a** (two cases): four points sharing a metric but differing in `asset` or `ts`
must all survive, and a cross-participant collision must dedup on the genuine triple while a
same-metric-different-hour point survives. Re-run after: M3 → `2 failed`, M6 → `1 failed`,
M7 → `2 failed`.

### Honest gap in this entry

`test/registry.merge.test.ts`'s header says the Phase-1 red run was "captured verbatim" here. **It
was not** — the building agent was interrupted before writing this section, and the transcript is
lost. Re-deriving it after the fact would be a reconstruction presented as a capture, which is the
defect this journal exists to prevent, so it is recorded as missing instead. Everything else in this
entry was run by the orchestrator on the finished tree and pasted from real output.

Final: `test/registry.merge.test.ts` 18/18; suite 1569 → **1587** (core 1228 → 1246, mcp-server 341
unchanged), all six PLAN §0.2 gates green. `test/merge-activation.test.ts` changed by one test
(+23/−10): its rank probe used to read `tried[]` off a thrown `CapabilityUnavailableError`, but a
`merge: true` route now returns an empty merged success instead of throwing (that outcome branch is
013-5's), so the probe records `isAvailable()` call order directly — a stronger observation, not a
weakened one. No `docs/reports/` in this project; this entry is the durable record.

## T-013 task 013-5 (2026-08-08) — the terminal outcome contract: four branches, three participant states, and the deadline as a precondition on all of them

**What the merge walk gained, all of it AFTER the loop except two one-line flag writes inside it.**
The deadline precondition (`if (deadlineHit) throw new CapabilityDeadlineExceededError(...)`),
branch (c) (`missing.length > 0 && contributors.size === 0` → `CapabilityUnavailableError`), and
`missingSources`. Branches (a) and (b) are what remains when neither throw fires — deliberately not
written as explicit `if`s, because a branch with no code of its own is a branch that cannot drift
from the one above it.

**The two writes that DO live inside the loop are the two deadline doors**, and 013-4's own comment
predicted them: door 1 is `deadlineHit = true` in the per-adapter pre-check, door 2 is
`if (error instanceof DeadlineExceededError) deadlineHit = true;` in the `fetch()` catch. Before
this task the merge path had NEITHER — the task file marked door 2 🔴 as missing, and it was.
`hadFailure` stays unwritten: the merge walk classifies a missing participant structurally from
`skipped[].kind` and never consults that flag, so writing it would still be the dead store
`no-useless-assignment` catches.

**`missingSources` relays, never string-matches.** `skipped[].kind` DECIDES that a participant is
missing; `tried[].reason` only supplies the message once that decision is made. That is the debt
013-4's own roast named (F-7) and the reason `skipped` was added there rather than here. `'chain'`
is the one kind with nothing to relay — the chain-scoped skip writes no `tried[]` entry on purpose
— so its reason is synthesized.

**One addition to the branch-(c) throw that is not merely wiring.** The task file requires `tried`
to name BOTH participants on UC-19, and it did not: a participant that answers with an empty array
satisfies the default `{kind:'any'}` policy, contributes nothing, and leaves no `tried` entry at
all. So the throw now appends `'answered, but with zero points for this window'` for every answerer
not already named. Written at the throw and nowhere else: on branches (a)/(b) an empty answer is an
ordinary fact, and it becomes worth reporting exactly when it is the reason the call is failing.

### Mutation protocol — six mutants, run and captured. **One survived, and closing it changed the code.**

Every mutant applied by exact-string replacement (a helper that REFUSES on 0 or >1 matches — see
the last row for why that mattered), run, then reverted; tree re-verified after each.

| #   | Mutation                                                               | Result                                                  |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| M1  | door 2: drop `if (error instanceof DeadlineExceededError) …`           | `× TC-INT-07` — 1 failed                                |
| M2  | door 1: drop `deadlineHit = true` from the merge pre-check             | `× TC-INT-06` — 1 failed                                |
| M3  | branch (c): disable the throw                                          | `× TC-INT-04`, `× TC-INT-10` — 2 failed                 |
| M4  | B-1: disable the `perSourceCache.length === 0` throw                   | **`16 passed` — SURVIVED**                              |
| M5  | `missingSources`: relay from `tried[]` for the `'chain'` kind too      | `× TC-INT-15` — 1 failed                                |
| M6  | PLAN §0.10: drop `\|\| Date.now() >= effectiveDeadlineAtMs` (terminal) | `× TC-INT-11b`, `× TC-OQ6-d` — 1 file failed, 68 passed |

**M6 is the mutation PLAN §0.10 assigns to this task, and it killed exactly the test the task file
predicted** (`TC-INT-11b` in `registry.deadline.test.ts` — the adapter that swallows the typed
class), plus `TC-OQ6-d`. Two things worth keeping about how it went:

- The task file warned that this coordinate had already pointed at a COMMENT once, so the target was
  found by its quoted text. It had moved again: `:1554` in the task file, `:1630` in the tree.
- **The revert refused, and the refusal was the safety net working.** After the mutation the file
  held TWO occurrences of `if (deadlineHit) {` — the mutant and this task's own precondition — so
  the exact-string helper would not guess which to restore. Reverted by hand against the surrounding
  lines. A blind `sed` would have silently rewritten the precondition instead.

**Why M4 survived, and what it means.** Two unavailable participants reach branch (c) as well
(`missing.length > 0`, `contributors` empty), so B-1 and (c) each catch what the other catches, and
TC-INT-05 pins the OUTCOME rather than either mechanism. A case to isolate B-1 was attempted — a
merge route with an empty `adapterIds`, the one shape branch (c) structurally cannot catch, and one
PLAN §0.5 item 2 records as constructible. **It never reaches the merge walk:** GATE 2 refuses it
with `CapabilityNotCoveredOnChainError`, because a route with no adapters covers no chain. Run, not
reasoned — the assertion named that class.

So: **after this task B-1 is unreachable.** It is kept as a second net over `source: ''` (AC-47 is
unconditional) and both the code comment and `TC-INT-05`'s docstring now say so in those words,
instead of the previous claim that it was the thing standing between the caller and a hollow
success. An unreachable guard believed to be load-bearing is how the next reader builds on sand.

### Honest gap in this entry

**These 15 cases were never run against a phase-1 skeleton, and no such red transcript exists.**
This task was resumed from an interrupted run whose working tree already carried the implementation
(the precondition, branch (c), `missingSources`), so "tests first" was not available to do. The
substitute is the mutation table above, and it is stronger than the missing artifact rather than a
stand-in for it: a red-first run proves a case CAN fail, while a targeted mutation proves it fails
for the stated reason. Every case carries its `KILLED_BY:` line naming that mutation. Recorded as
missing rather than reconstructed — the same rule 013-4's entry applied to itself.

**A number in the 013-4 entry above is wrong and is left standing with this correction.** It records
`core 1228 → 1246`. Measured on the clean tree at `681e372` before this task touched anything:
**core 68 files / 1260 tests**. Every commit after `e95b909` is docs-only, so 1246 was never true of
a finished 013-4 tree; it was recorded mid-run. The baseline this task is measured against is the
one that was measured, not the one that was written down.

Final: `test/registry.merge-outcomes.test.ts` 15/15; core 68 files/1260 → **69 files/1275**, suite
1601 → **1616** (mcp-server 341 unchanged, no diff attributable to this task). All six PLAN §0.2
gates green: `lint`, `format:check`, `typecheck`, `test`, `build`, `smoke:dist`. `usage` untouched —
every case runs on mock adapters, zero network. No `docs/reports/` in this project; this entry is the
durable record.

## T-013 task 013-6 (2026-08-09) — merge activated on the two real routes, and the dedup rule narrowed to cross-participant only

Activation itself is two `merge: true` flags and a per-route comment. The task's own pre-flight note
("check the `pg-history` limiter BEFORE activating") and an adversarial pass on the result are what
made this task larger than that.

### 1. The limiter, re-derived rather than documented (owner decision, option (a))

`{capacity: 2, refillPerSec: 0.2}` — one token per five seconds — was sized for a SPARE LEG. Before
merge, `pg-history` was reached only when `platform-explorer` threw, which the route's default
`{kind: 'any'}` policy made almost never. After activation every merged cache-miss takes a token
from one per-provider bucket shared by both merge capabilities: ~12 merged calls/minute, hidden by
the 3 600 s TTL right up until an agent varies its arguments.

Re-derived to `{capacity: 10, refillPerSec: 5}` (defillama's pair — the most generous free entry).
The justification is not "more is better": every other free adapter is a third-party host with a
quota to respect, and `pg-history` is our own Postgres, so its bucket is a runaway guard rather than
a contract with a vendor.

Five sites, because a constant that lives in two places drifts: `providers.config.ts` (the value +
the arithmetic), `adapters/pg-history/index.ts` (the docstring that explained the old pair),
`docs/architectures/system-architecture.md` (the mirrored config block),
`test/pg-history.contract.test.ts` (two saturation tests that hard-coded "2 priming calls" — one
would have gone red, **the other would have stayed green while testing nothing**), and
`capability-manifest.ts` (a pointer, because that file derives the E-PG deadline envelope; the
envelope is unaffected — its addend is `MAX_WAIT_MS`, a constant of the limiter, not of the bucket).

### 2. The finding: the hour was the identity of a POINT

| Capability                      | single-winner | merged, as first built | merged, after the fix |
| ------------------------------- | ------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------- |
| `platform.metrics.history`      | 12            | **2**                  | 15                    |
| `privacy.shielded_pool.history` | 12            | **2**                  | 13                    |

`platform-explorer` samples every ~5 minutes, so 11 of its 12 points shared an hour with a sibling
and were discarded — points from ONE participant, which are distinct observations, not duplicates.
On `privacy.shielded_pool.history` the collapse was purely destructive: the two participants write
DIFFERENT metrics, so no slot was ever contested there at all.

Measured, not reasoned: a throwaway probe resolved both capabilities against the shipped route table
and again against the same table with `merge` stripped off, and printed both counts.

**The DB precedent 013-4 cited is narrowed, not withdrawn.**
`ON CONFLICT (source, asset, metric, ts_bucket)` leads with `source` — one row per SOURCE per hour,
not one row per hour. Authority for collapsing an hour across sources; none for collapsing one
source's own hour.

**Owner decision 2026-08-09** (R-161(f)): dedup applies only BETWEEN participants. The highest-rank
participant contributes every point it answered with; a lower-rank one contributes only into slots
nobody above it filled. `claimedSlots` is committed after each participant, never during — a
participant must not suppress its own second point in an hour, which is the same bug one level down.

This is a retro-edit to `013-4`'s territory (`e95b909`), recorded in PLAN §0.4a with its boundary:
`mergeInto` and the accumulator changed; walk order, per-adapter cache, policy site and 013-5's
outcome contract did not. Full suite after: core 1282 / mcp-server 341 — the same counts as before
the change, so **no existing test relied on within-participant collapse**.

### 3. Why a green suite did not catch it

`merge-routes.test.ts` asserted on `new Set(metricsOf(...))` — the SET of labels, which a collapse
does not change — and made no assertion about point COUNT anywhere. The test was built around the
overlap case and passed for the right reason on the wrong axis. AC-23 now requires the count stated
as a number, and TC-INT-01/02 assert: the rank-1 participant's full fixture length, the losing
value's absence by `valueRaw`, the exact set of metrics relayed from `pg-history`, and the total.

### Left open, and it is T-016's

The DB key admits two rows for one metric in one hour under different `source` values. Both now pass
(no within-participant dedup). Whether such pairs exist for `identities_total` in the live ledger
was NOT measured — stated as unmeasured rather than assumed either way. Ranking rows by `source` is
`metrics.source_priority`, T-016's mechanism (OD-T013-2(b)).

Final: `test/merge-routes.test.ts` 7/7; core **70 files / 1282 tests**, mcp-server 341, suite
**1623**. All six PLAN §0.2 gates green. Zero network, zero credits — both merge participants are
free and every case runs on fixtures.

## T-013 task 013-9 (2026-08-09) — three D5 deviations recorded, and every boundary measured rather than asserted

### The ADR-002 changelog carries THREE entries, not two

R-181 was written for two. A third was found during 013-6 and is recorded as its own entry rather
than folded into the existing pair — which is what this task's own notes require: "Третье
отклонение, найденное при исполнении, — находка задачи: оно записывается отдельной записью и
выносится на решение владельца, а не растворяется в существующих двух."

| #   | Deviation                                                                                           | Date       |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- |
| 1   | conflict rank is compiled (`adapterIds` order), not `source_priority`                               | 2026-08-05 |
| 2   | outcome distinction EXTENDED, not applied "verbatim" — three participant states, branches (b)/(c)   | 2026-08-05 |
| 3   | dedup applies only BETWEEN participants; the hour is a cross-participant slot, not a point identity | 2026-08-09 |

Three footnotes attach them to the literal text they deviate from — two on the `series` row of the
`shape` table (rank, dedup) and one on the partial-failure paragraph (outcomes). **The literal text
stays visible beside each footnote**, the R-156(d) technique T-012 used on D4 п.5. The "Влияние на
этапы" table is untouched — verified by `git diff`, not by intent.

### Boundaries: gates with positive controls, because a gate that cannot fail proves nothing

- **`source_priority`** — 0 occurrences across `packages/core/src` + `packages/mcp-server/src`.
  **Positive control executed**: one occurrence injected, the grep found exactly 1, the injection
  removed, `git diff` confirms the tree is unchanged. Without that control the zero would be
  indistinguishable from a grep that searches nothing.
- **`.trust`** — `tier-single-source.test.ts` green (14 tests); no reader outside `adapters/types.ts`.
- **T-016 subject matter across the whole `681e372..HEAD` range** — `segmentBy`, `sourceTrust`,
  `communityMark`, `trustSegment`: zero. `source → trust` appears twice and `.trust` once, all three
  in PLAN/TASK prose that NAMES the boundary as out of scope. Inspected line by line rather than
  counted: a boundary being recorded is the opposite of a boundary being crossed.
- **`entity.labels`** — neither eligibility (`mergeable` absent from its manifest row) nor
  activation (`merge` absent from its route). Both measured by reading the shipped files.

### `usage` ledger

`~/.onchain-intel/cache.sqlite3`, `SELECT COUNT(*), SUM(credits_used) FROM usage` — **0 rows, 0
credits before the run and after it.** Both merge participants are free, every test runs on fixtures
or mock adapters, and the one network call this milestone permits (R-173) belongs to 013-10 and has
not run. The ledger being empty rather than merely unchanged is worth stating: there is no prior
balance masking a small increment.
